- issue-004: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: The ontology permits adjusted Exposure.risk_grade values but does not represent derived versus adjusted state, precedence, source value, actor, time, reason, or approval evidence. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / classification_rules: Exposure.risk_grade manual adjustment Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: classification_rules[0] says Exposure.risk_grade is derived from RiskRating.grade but the reporting team may manually adjust it based on market conditions; Exposure.risk_grade has only the adjusted enum value and no adjustment evidence attributes or entity. Source finding context: Manual risk-grade overrides are in scope but the ontology lacks audit evidence concepts for the override action. Source finding context: A shared concept authority for a risk engine and reporting system must let both systems distinguish engine-derived grades from manually adjusted reported grades. The target explicitly allows manual adjustment, while the only modeled grade-bearing attributes are Borrower.internal_rating, RiskRating.grade, and Exposure.risk_grade. None of the entities or relations capture an override action, actor, timestamp, rationale, approval, or prior/current grade pair, so the reporting value cannot be reconstructed or audited from the ontology itself. Source finding context: Add a bounded override/audit concept such as RiskGradeAdjustment with exposure_ref, source_rating_ref or source_grade, adjusted_grade, actor, adjusted_at, reason/market_basis, approval_ref/status, and effective period; relate Exposure.risk_grade to the latest effective approved adjustment or the derived RiskRating mapping when no adjustment exists. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology is intended as a shared concept authority for the risk engine and reporting system. Source finding context: When Exposure.risk_grade is manually changed by the reporting team, downstream users cannot tell whether a reported grade is engine-derived or adjusted, nor reconstruct the actor, time, and basis for the reported value. Source finding context: This weakens auditability and cross-system trust for a core risk classification value, especially because the target itself authorizes manual changes to that value. Source finding context: The ontology models adjusted risk grade as a bare Exposure attribute while placing the manual adjustment rule only in free-text classification_rules. Source finding context: Exposure.risk_grade is represented as a single enum attribute on Exposure. Source finding context: The classification rule permits the reporting team to manually adjust that value based on market conditions. Source finding context: No entity or attribute captures the manual adjustment action, actor, timestamp, basis, approval, or source-versus-adjusted value.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-005: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Ratings, limits, mappings, and exchange rates are time-sensitive but selectively or not temporally qualified, making historical calculations and reports non-reproducible. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / rating and exchange-rate dependent values Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: RiskRating.grade has no effective date; Borrower.internal_rating has no time fields; Exposure.risk_grade has no effective date; classification_rules[1] uses each system's same-day exchange-rate table, but no exchange-rate concept exists. Source finding context: Time-dependent classification inputs are modeled without validity periods or history, preventing point-in-time reconstruction. Source finding context: The review target is a shared concept basis for risk calculation and reporting. For historical reports or replayed risk-engine decisions, both systems need the value that was valid at the relevant point in time. The target explicitly mentions monthly batch aggregate timing and same-day exchange-rate tables, but the ontology lacks effective_from/effective_to, as_of, version, or source-table references for several time-dependent inputs, so historical utilization and grade outputs cannot be reconstructed from the concept model. Source finding context: Introduce temporal validity consistently for time-dependent concepts: add effective_from/effective_to or as_of/version fields to RiskRating, Borrower rating, Exposure.risk_grade, Limit.approved_amount, and any mapping table; add an ExchangeRate or CurrencyConversionRate concept with source_system/table authority, currency pair, rate_date/time, and rate value. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002 Source finding context: The ontology is meant to support shared risk-engine and reporting interpretation of credit-risk values. Source finding context: For a prior reporting date or risk decision, the systems need to reconstruct the rating, limit, mapping, and exchange-rate values valid at that time, but the ontology only stores current or undated values for several inputs. Source finding context: This can make historical reports non-reproducible and risk calculations inconsistent across systems because point-in-time authority is absent for core classification inputs. Source finding context: Temporal coverage is applied only selectively rather than as a concept dimension for all point-in-time risk inputs. Source finding context: Some concepts include point-in-time markers, such as Collateral.appraised_at and ExposureAggregate.as_of. Source finding context: Other time-dependent values, including ratings, risk grades, approved limits, and exchange rates, lack equivalent validity/history fields or entities. Source finding context: Because classification and utilization calculations depend on those values, historical outputs cannot be reconstructed from the ontology alone.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-006: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - rationale: This is the broad authority root behind several consumer-reconciliation risks: no canonical owner, version, source, precedence, or conflict-resolution concept is modeled. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / multi-system managed values and grade scales Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: RiskRating.grade note sends mapping to a risk-team wiki; Limit.approved_amount note says approval system records it and CRM available_limit is managed together; classification_rules[1] uses each system's exchange-rate table; notes say each consuming system performs grade-scale conversion. Source finding context: Values explicitly shared across systems lack a single authority concept, leaving consumers to reconcile competing sources themselves. Source finding context: Coverage of single authority is required when values are managed across systems. The target's declared purpose is to be a shared concept authority, but several fields are explicitly delegated to external or per-consumer sources without an authoritative entity, relation, or precedence rule. That omission means the risk engine and reporting system can legally produce different grade mappings, limit utilization, or currency-converted values while still conforming to the ontology text. Source finding context: Add authority-bearing concepts or attributes for each parallel value: a canonical RatingScaleMapping with owner/version/effective period; a LimitAuthority or LimitSnapshot separating approved_amount from CRM available_limit with source_system and precedence; and a governed ExchangeRateSource/ExchangeRate table. State which source controls each calculation and how conflicts are resolved. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-003 Source finding context: The artifact declares itself the shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When systems disagree on grade conversion, available limit, or exchange rate, the ontology provides no authoritative source or precedence rule to resolve the discrepancy. Source finding context: The shared ontology fails its authority purpose for central risk/reporting calculations and allows inconsistent but ontology-compliant outputs across consuming systems. Source finding context: The ontology acknowledges cross-system coexistence but does not promote mapping, limit availability, or exchange-rate authority into governed ontology concepts. Source finding context: The target declares multiple system-specific sources for grade conversion, limits, and exchange rates. Source finding context: No entity or relation names a canonical source, precedence rule, owner, version, or conflict-resolution concept for those values. Source finding context: Therefore the risk engine and reporting system can each use different external mappings or rates while remaining inside the documented ontology.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-012: fix_now
  - rationale: This is the semantic form of the Collateral inheritance defect: downstream systems could count, filter, validate, or report collateral under exposure semantics. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml entities.Collateral Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Collateral.definition = "익스포저를 담보하는 자산. 담보 평가의 기본 단위." and Collateral.is_a = Exposure Source finding context: Collateral is semantically misclassified as an Exposure subtype. Source finding context: The embedded target defines Exposure as credit exposure to a borrower, while Collateral is defined as an asset that secures an exposure. These are different ontological types: a credit obligation/position versus a securing asset. Because the artifact is intended as a shared concept authority for risk engines and reporting systems, this subtype assertion can cause consumers to inherit exposure semantics for collateral records and misinterpret collateral assets as exposure instances. Source finding context: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure`; keep Collateral as a separate entity and express the relationship through `Exposure secured_by Collateral` or an equivalent association with explicit cardinality/role. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's declared purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems covering exposures, grades, limits, and collateral. Source finding context: When a consuming engine or report treats subtype assertions as inheritance or classification authority, Collateral becomes an Exposure instance despite the definitions distinguishing collateral assets from credit exposures. Source finding context: This weakens trust in the shared taxonomy because a foundational entity boundary is semantically wrong, allowing collateral records to be counted, filtered, validated, or reported under exposure semantics. Source finding context: The ontology uses subtype inheritance for Collateral even though its own definitions only support an associative securing relationship to Exposure. Source finding context: Collateral is asserted as a subtype of Exposure via `is_a: Exposure`. Source finding context: The definitions distinguish Exposure as a credit exposure to a borrower and Collateral as an asset that secures an exposure. Source finding context: The same model already has an associative relation `Exposure secured_by Collateral`, so the subtype assertion is not needed to express the securing relationship.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-013: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - rationale: The issue captures the semantic grade-authority problem behind divergent conversion and reporting behavior, overlapping with but distinct from the structural conversion-authority issue. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml rating concepts Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Borrower.internal_rating values are described as 1~10; RiskRating.grade values are [R1, R2, R3, R4, R5]; Exposure.risk_grade values are [AAA, AA, A, BBB, BB, B, CCC]; notes say the three scales coexist and each consuming system performs conversion. Source finding context: Three credit-grade concepts are semantically overlapping but lack an authoritative mapping inside the ontology. Source finding context: All three fields name a borrower/exposure credit risk grade, but their scale names and authorities differ. The target itself says this artifact is meant to be the shared concept authority between a risk engine and reporting systems; leaving semantically overlapping grades to separate consumer-side mappings means the same borrower or exposure risk meaning can diverge by system. Source finding context: Define canonical grade concepts and authority boundaries in the ontology: either model one canonical rating with explicit scale mappings/versioning, or split the concepts by meaning such as borrower internal rating, engine borrower rating, and report exposure grade with authoritative mapping rules and allowed override semantics. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002 Source finding context: Shared concept authority for the risk engine and reporting system, especially for ratings used in classification and reports. Source finding context: When the engine emits RiskRating.grade and reporting consumes Exposure.risk_grade or Borrower.internal_rating, each consumer may apply a different conversion or manual override without an ontology-governed semantic mapping. Source finding context: The ontology cannot reliably establish whether two systems are referring to the same risk grade meaning, so risk classification and reporting can disagree while still appearing compliant with the shared taxonomy. Source finding context: The ontology defines multiple overlapping rating scales but delegates conversion authority to external wiki/consumer systems instead of making the semantic mapping part of the shared concept model. Source finding context: Borrower.internal_rating, RiskRating.grade, and Exposure.risk_grade all represent credit or risk grade concepts with different scales. Source finding context: The classification rule derives Exposure.risk_grade from RiskRating.grade but also allows report-team manual adjustment based on market conditions. Source finding context: The notes state that conversion among the three coexisting scales is performed by each consuming system.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-003: needs_evidence
  - rationale: The deliberated claim is not that the ontology must own the status internally, but that the boundary relation is missing for a purpose-critical external concept. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / scope notes excluding default status Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: notes[1] says default/delinquency status is outside this ontology because a separate delinquency-management system handles it; the header says the ontology is for credit-risk management and shared risk/reporting concepts. Source finding context: Excluding default status from a credit-risk shared ontology leaves a purpose-critical stakeholder boundary undefined. Source finding context: Boundary exclusions can be valid, but for credit risk management, default/delinquency status is central to the meaning and operational use of risk grades, exposure control, and reports. A shared ontology can delegate ownership to another system while still defining the boundary concept and relation. Here the exclusion is accepted without a compensating interface, which risks purpose drift: the ontology optimizes scope neatness over the completeness needed for shared risk interpretation. Source finding context: Keep delinquency/default ownership outside if needed, but add a boundary concept or relation such as `DefaultStatus`/`DelinquencyStatus` with source authority, join key, as-of time, and effects on ratings, exposure classification, limit compliance, and reporting eligibility. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-003 Source finding context: The ontology's declared credit-risk management purpose and its role as a shared basis for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When a borrower or exposure has delinquency/default state in the external system, but risk engine or reporting consumers rely on this ontology without a defined boundary relation to that state. Source finding context: Core credit-risk outputs can omit or inconsistently join a purpose-critical status, weakening shared interpretation across stakeholders even if another system owns the source data. Source finding context: The ontology treats an ownership boundary as a scope exclusion instead of modeling the boundary relation for a purpose-critical risk concept. Source finding context: The ontology is scoped as a credit-risk management concept model for shared engine/reporting use. Source finding context: The ontology excludes default/delinquency status entirely because a separate system handles it. Source finding context: No visible entity or relation in the bounded target links borrower, exposure, rating, limit, or report concepts to the external default/delinquency status. Source finding context: This leaves a core risk-status boundary undefined for systems expected to share concepts through the ontology.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-007: needs_evidence
  - rationale: The issue remains material but depends on an owner decision: either model an externally owned lifecycle-status boundary or explicitly state that this ontology must not be used to consume that status. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / credit risk domain scope Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: notes[1] excludes delinquency/default status as outside scope; entity list contains Borrower, Exposure, Collateral, RiskRating, Limit, ExposureAggregate, RiskAppetite only. Source finding context: Default/delinquency risk status is excluded despite being a central credit-risk lifecycle area, leaving the ontology under-covered for credit-risk reporting. Source finding context: From a coverage perspective, a credit-risk classification ontology shared by risk and reporting needs at least a conceptual place for adverse credit states, even if operational ownership belongs to another system. The target defines exposure, ratings, limits, collateral, aggregate exposure, and risk appetite, but omits the lifecycle/outcome states that determine whether credit risk has materialized. Because the note makes the omission intentional, the gap is inside the declared target boundary rather than merely unknown external detail. Source finding context: Add a minimal externally-owned CreditRiskStatus or DelinquencyDefaultStatus projection with borrower/exposure reference, status enum, source_system=delinquency management, as_of/effective period, and lifecycle states such as current, delinquent, defaulted, cured, written_off, closed, or explicitly define why reports and engines must never consume that status through this ontology. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004 Source finding context: The ontology presents itself as a credit-risk classification model for lending risk management and shared risk/reporting concepts. Source finding context: Risk reports or engine classifications that need to combine ratings/exposure with delinquency or default status cannot do so through the shared ontology. Source finding context: The ontology covers pre-default exposure and rating concepts but lacks a central outcome/lifecycle dimension, which can fragment credit-risk reporting across systems. Source finding context: The ontology treats system ownership of delinquency/default handling as a reason to omit the concept from the shared classification model. Source finding context: The target explicitly states delinquency/default status is outside the ontology scope because another system handles it. Source finding context: No replacement projection, reference, or lifecycle status concept is modeled in the entity or relation set. Source finding context: As a result, the shared credit-risk model cannot cover materialized credit-risk state across the risk/reporting boundary.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-008: follow_up
  - rationale: The absence of lifecycle state or event history leaves inclusion, exclusion, closure, release, and correction behavior to consumers rather than the shared ontology. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / lifecycle coverage for limits, exposures, collateral, and risk appetite Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: entities define Limit.approved_amount, Exposure.amount/product_type, Collateral.appraised_value/appraised_at, RiskAppetite.compliance_status, but no lifecycle states or termination/archive/disposal fields. Source finding context: Stateful credit-risk objects lack lifecycle coverage from creation through closure, expiry, archival, or post-close correction. Source finding context: Lifecycle coverage matters for shared risk/reporting concepts because calculations and reports often distinguish active exposure from closed exposure, approved limit from expired limit, pledged collateral from released collateral, and current breach from remediated breach. The target has a compliance_status enum for RiskAppetite and an appraised_at timestamp for Collateral, but no lifecycle model for the objects whose states affect inclusion, aging, and correction behavior. Source finding context: Add lifecycle/status attributes or separate lifecycle event concepts for Exposure, Limit, Collateral, and RiskAppetiteCompliance, including effective timestamps, terminal states, and correction/reissue events after a reporting period closes. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-005 Source finding context: The ontology is meant to provide shared concepts for risk engine calculations and reporting outputs. Source finding context: When an exposure closes, a limit expires, collateral is released/revalued, or a breach is remediated/corrected after reporting close, the ontology has no concept to represent the state transition or terminal condition. Source finding context: Risk engines and reports may include or exclude objects inconsistently because the shared taxonomy does not cover lifecycle state or post-terminal corrections. Source finding context: The ontology represents risk objects as current-value records instead of lifecycle-bearing records or event histories. Source finding context: The entity attributes emphasize current values and identifiers for Exposure, Limit, Collateral, and RiskAppetite. Source finding context: Lifecycle states and lifecycle events are absent for those entities, except for the narrow compliant/breached status on RiskAppetite. Source finding context: This absence prevents consistent representation of active versus terminal, remediated, released, or corrected records.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-009: follow_up
  - rationale: Closed enums are not inherently wrong, but for changing product, collateral, segment, and grade classifications they enable schema-change churn and weaken versioned identity. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / closed categorical enums Source finding context: materialized-input.md: Borrower.segment enum values [retail, sme, corporate]; Exposure.product_type enum values [term_loan, revolving, guarantee]; Collateral.collateral_type enum values [real_estate, deposit, securities]; RiskRating.grade and Exposure.risk_grade also use closed value lists. Source finding context: 핵심 분류값들이 닫힌 enum으로만 표현되어 새 상품·담보·차주 세그먼트 추가가 데이터 확장이 아니라 온톨로지 스키마 변경이 된다. Source finding context: 여신 리스크 분류체계는 새 상품, 새 담보 유형, 새 고객 세그먼트를 지속적으로 받아들여야 한다. 현재 구조에서는 예컨대 정책금융 상품, 파생 보증, 지식재산 담보, 플랫폼 사업자 세그먼트 같은 새 범주가 생길 때 기존 속성 정의를 직접 수정해야 한다. 이 방식은 기존 데이터의 과거 코드 의미와 새 코드 의미를 함께 유지하기 어렵고, 리스크 엔진/보고 시스템 동시 배포 요구를 만든다. Source finding context: enum 값 목록을 ProductType, CollateralType, BorrowerSegment, GradeCode 같은 코드셋 엔티티로 분리하고 code, label, definition, parent_code, status, effective_from/effective_to, source_authority를 둔다. 속성은 enum literal 대신 코드셋 참조를 사용하게 한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-003 Source finding context: 여신 리스크 분류 온톨로지의 확장성과 리스크 엔진/보고 시스템 간 분류 기준 공유. Source finding context: 새 상품 유형, 담보 유형, 차주 세그먼트, 등급 코드가 추가되거나 기존 코드가 폐기될 때 속성 enum 자체를 수정해야 한다. Source finding context: 분류 변경이 데이터/코드셋 관리가 아니라 스키마 변경과 소비자 배포 문제가 되어 변경 비용과 호환성 위험이 커진다. Source finding context: 변경 가능한 업무 분류값을 버전 관리되는 코드셋 개념이 아니라 속성 내부의 닫힌 enum literal로 모델링한다. Source finding context: 주요 분류 속성이 고정 enum 값 목록으로 선언되어 있다. Source finding context: enum 값에 대한 유효기간, 상태, 상하위 관계, 외부 코드 매핑을 담는 별도 분류 엔티티가 보이지 않는다. Source finding context: 새 범주 수용 시 기존 ontology schema의 속성 값 목록 수정이 필요하다.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-010: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: The model simultaneously uses inheritance and association for Collateral versus Exposure, creating a concrete type-boundary contradiction. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / entities.Collateral Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `Collateral.definition` states collateral is an asset securing an exposure, while `Collateral.is_a: Exposure` declares Collateral as a subtype of Exposure. Source finding context: `Collateral` is simultaneously defined as a securing asset and declared to be an `Exposure`, creating an explicit class-membership conflict. Source finding context: The contradiction is internal to the present claim set: `Exposure` is defined as credit exposure to a borrower, while `Collateral` is defined as an asset that secures that exposure. The relation `Exposure -> Collateral` (`secured_by`) also treats them as separate roles. For a shared concept authority between a risk engine and reporting system, this weakens classification because collateral records can be logically admitted wherever exposure records are expected. Source finding context: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure` and model collateral only as a separate entity related to `Exposure` through `secured_by`, or introduce a separate parent such as `RiskMitigant` if inheritance is needed. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When systems interpret `is_a` as subtype inheritance or type membership, collateral instances become valid exposure instances despite being separately defined as securing assets. Source finding context: Risk engines or reports can include collateral objects in exposure-oriented logic, aggregations, or validations, undermining the shared classification contract. Source finding context: The ontology uses subtype inheritance (`is_a`) to connect a role/entity that is explicitly defined as a separate secured asset rather than a credit exposure. Source finding context: `Collateral` is declared as a subtype of `Exposure` through `is_a: Exposure`. Source finding context: `Collateral` is also defined as an asset that secures an exposure, not as the exposure itself. Source finding context: The relation set separately links `Exposure` to `Collateral` with `secured_by`, reinforcing that the claims treat them as distinct roles while inheritance collapses them into one type hierarchy.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-011: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Only total_amount and threshold are comparable numeric attributes; the current prose forces consumers to infer projections and scope. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / RiskAppetite.definition Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `RiskAppetite.definition`: `ExposureAggregate가 RiskAppetite를 초과하면 RiskAppetite 위반이며, 위반 여부는 RiskAppetite 준수 상태로 정의된다`; attributes include `threshold` and `compliance_status`, but no amount-valued RiskAppetite field. Source finding context: The breach rule compares `ExposureAggregate` to `RiskAppetite` as an entity, while the comparable numeric attribute is `RiskAppetite.threshold`. Source finding context: The present claims declare `ExposureAggregate.total_amount` and `RiskAppetite.threshold` as numbers, but not `ExposureAggregate` or `RiskAppetite` themselves as numeric values. A greater-than comparison between an aggregate entity and an appetite entity is not simultaneously satisfiable with the declared attribute typing unless an unstated projection is assumed. In a shared engine/reporting contract, this makes the compliance rule ambiguous at the formal level where the engine needs an executable predicate and reports need the same predicate. Source finding context: Rewrite the rule as an attribute-level predicate, e.g. `RiskAppetite.compliance_status = breached iff ExposureAggregate.total_amount > RiskAppetite.threshold` for the applicable borrower/scope/as_of, and define the required relation/scope between the aggregate and appetite. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-002 Source finding context: Shared definition of risk appetite breach/compliance between risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When consumers implement the breach predicate from the ontology text, the rule compares entity objects rather than declared numeric attributes. Source finding context: Different consumers may choose different implicit projections or reject the rule as invalid, so breach status can diverge between engine and reports. Source finding context: The rule is stated at entity level even though the ontology declares the comparable quantities only as attributes. Source finding context: `RiskAppetite.definition` states the violation condition as `ExposureAggregate` exceeding `RiskAppetite`. Source finding context: The ontology declares the numeric measure for the aggregate as `ExposureAggregate.total_amount`, not as the `ExposureAggregate` entity itself. Source finding context: The ontology declares the numeric appetite value as `RiskAppetite.threshold`, not as the `RiskAppetite` entity itself.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-014: follow_up, accept_risk, fix_before_release
  - rationale: The core problem is an unresolved meaning choice: if derived, LTV needs formula and source semantics; if manual, it needs provenance and naming or boundary clarity. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml Exposure.ltv Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Exposure.ltv note = "담보인정비율. 심사 시점에 심사역이 입력한다."; Collateral has appraised_value and classification rule covers stale appraisals. Source finding context: LTV is named as a derived ratio but modeled as an independent manual input without source-value semantics. Source finding context: The semantics lens treats names containing ratios as derived-value risk areas when source attributes coexist. Here Collateral.appraised_value and appraised_at are present, and stale valuation affects reevaluation, but LTV is independently entered. This creates semantic drift: consumers cannot know whether LTV means current exposure-to-current-collateral value, origination-time ratio, approved collateral recognition ratio, or a manually overridden review metric. Source finding context: Either define LTV as a derived attribute with an authoritative formula, source fields, valuation timestamp, and currency basis, or rename/split it into an explicitly manual review metric with override provenance and as-of semantics. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003 Source finding context: Shared risk-engine and reporting concept basis for exposure and collateral metrics. Source finding context: When systems compare or recompute LTV using Exposure.amount and Collateral.appraised_value/appraised_at, the stored manual LTV may not mean the same ratio or time basis. Source finding context: Risk engines and reports can disagree on collateral adequacy or policy thresholds because the ontology exposes a ratio-shaped concept without authoritative derivation or temporal semantics. Source finding context: A ratio-named field is modeled as an independent manual input while likely source values and valuation timing are also modeled. Source finding context: `ltv` is defined as a number and described as a collateral recognition ratio manually entered at review time. Source finding context: The ontology also contains Exposure.amount and Collateral.appraised_value/appraised_at, which are plausible source and timing inputs for an LTV-like ratio. Source finding context: Collateral valuation age is semantically material because stale appraised_value triggers reevaluation after one year.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-015: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: The same utilization and limit terms can denote different denominator and currency-conversion meanings, allowing divergent control and reporting outcomes. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml Limit.approved_amount and classification_rules Limit 소진율 Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Limit.approved_amount note says CRM available_limit is managed by summing; classification rule defines utilization as ExposureAggregate.total_amount / Limit.approved_amount with each system's same-day FX table. Source finding context: Limit amount semantics mix approved limit, available limit, utilization denominator, and cross-system currency conversion without a single authoritative meaning. Source finding context: Approved amount, available limit, and utilized/exposure amount are distinct credit limit concepts. Treating CRM available_limit as summable with approved_amount blurs remaining-capacity semantics with approved-capacity semantics. Because utilization depends on both denominator meaning and FX basis, allowing each system to use its own FX table further changes the semantic value of the same named utilization ratio. Source finding context: Separate approved limit, available/remaining limit, and utilized exposure as distinct concepts; define whether they may be aggregated and under what currency/as-of rules. Make the FX source and valuation date an ontology-governed authority or explicit input, not a per-system semantic choice. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004 Source finding context: Shared concept authority for limits and utilization reporting between risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When systems calculate or report limit utilization for multi-currency or CRM-integrated borrowers, they may use different denominator semantics or FX sources under the same rule name. Source finding context: Limit breach, utilization, and risk appetite reporting can diverge while appearing to follow the ontology because the key terms do not have stable meanings. Source finding context: The ontology lacks semantic separation and authority rules for approved limit, available limit, utilized exposure, and FX conversion basis. Source finding context: Limit is defined as borrower/product credit limit, and approved_amount is the approved limit recorded by the approval system. Source finding context: The same approved_amount note introduces CRM available_limit and says it is managed by summing, mixing approved capacity with available remaining capacity semantics. Source finding context: Limit utilization is defined as ExposureAggregate.total_amount divided by Limit.approved_amount, with currency conversion performed using each system's same-day FX table.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-016: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: The missing relation is a graph-level symptom of the broader scoped compliance-model gap, but it is independently actionable because RiskAppetite cannot be traversed from the ontology structure. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml entities.RiskAppetite / relations Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:78-90 Source finding context: RiskAppetite is structurally orphaned despite being defined as a compliance concept over ExposureAggregate. Source finding context: The relations block connects Borrower, Exposure, Collateral, RiskRating, Limit, and ExposureAggregate, but omits RiskAppetite entirely. For an ontology intended as a shared concept authority between a risk engine and reporting systems, that leaves the breach/compliance concept outside the traversable model, so consumers cannot know structurally which aggregate, borrower segment, product scope, or portfolio scope an appetite threshold governs. Source finding context: Add explicit relation(s) connecting RiskAppetite to the governed aggregate or scope, for example ExposureAggregate -> RiskAppetite with a compares_to/governed_by kind, and include any needed scoping key such as borrower segment, portfolio, product, or currency if applicable. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's declared purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When consumers need to calculate or report RiskAppetite compliance from ExposureAggregate, the target contains no structural edge involving RiskAppetite. Source finding context: The compliance concept can be interpreted only from prose rather than from the ontology graph, weakening shared implementation and reporting alignment for a core risk-control concept. Source finding context: The ontology declares a RiskAppetite entity and prose comparison rule but does not add any relation involving RiskAppetite in the relations block. Source finding context: RiskAppetite is declared with threshold and compliance_status, and its definition depends on comparing ExposureAggregate to RiskAppetite. Source finding context: The complete visible relations block lists five relations and none has RiskAppetite as from or to.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-017: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - rationale: The missing edge is a material lineage facet of the broader grade-mapping authority problem; it should be handled with source path, join scope, precedence, and override semantics rather than as a bare relation alone. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml classification_rules[Exposure.risk_grade] / relations Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:24-34,48-57,85-93 Source finding context: The required derivation from RiskRating.grade to Exposure.risk_grade is not represented by a structural relation. Source finding context: Because the derivation is only in an unstructured rule string, consumers traversing the ontology graph cannot distinguish this required grade dependency from a loose shared-borrower association. That is structurally incomplete for a shared model where the risk engine produces RiskRating.grade and reports consume Exposure.risk_grade. Source finding context: Add an explicit relation or typed dependency for the derivation, such as RiskRating -> Exposure kind derives_risk_grade or Exposure.risk_grade derived_from RiskRating.grade, with the borrower join/scoping rule made explicit. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-002 Source finding context: The ontology's shared concept basis between risk engine output and reporting-system classification. Source finding context: When a consumer must determine the authoritative source path for Exposure.risk_grade, the graph lacks a direct derivation relation from RiskRating.grade. Source finding context: Risk-grade lineage becomes prose-only, so engine/report consumers can implement different joins or precedence behavior while still appearing to follow the ontology. Source finding context: A cross-entity derivation rule was added under classification_rules without a corresponding relation/dependency in the ontology graph. Source finding context: Exposure.risk_grade is declared as the exposure-level report grade. Source finding context: RiskRating.grade is declared as the risk-engine grade. Source finding context: The classification rule says Exposure.risk_grade is derived from RiskRating.grade, but the relations block has no direct relation between Exposure and RiskRating.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`

### Consensus (6/6, core-axis mode)
- issue-001 (high): Multiple rating scales coexist without an ontology-owned conversion authority, so shared grade meaning can diverge between risk-engine and reporting consumers while still appearing compliant with the ontology.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 2/2 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: axiology, evolution
  - remaining disagreement: 0/2 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: axiology, evolution
  - issue statement: The ontology lets multiple rating scales coexist without an ontology-owned conversion authority, weakening shared grade meaning and change continuity across risk engine and reporting consumers. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / classification_rules[0] and notes[0] Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: target header states the ontology is a shared authority document for risk engine and reports; classification_rules[0] says `Exposure.risk_grade` is derived from `RiskRating.grade` but can be manually adjusted by the reporting team; notes[0] says the three rating scales coexist and conversion is performed by each consuming system. Source finding context: Shared concept authority is weakened by delegating rating-scale conversion and report-grade override authority to consumers. Source finding context: For an ontology whose stated purpose is to provide shared conceptual authority, the rating concepts are purpose-critical because risk engines and reports both depend on them. Letting each consumer perform its own conversion, while also allowing one consumer group to override a derived grade, creates ungoverned local authority over a core risk classification. This is a value/purpose drift: local reporting flexibility is accepted without a declared governance seat, precedence rule, audit trail, or canonical mapping, so the shared basis can diverge exactly where shared meaning is most needed. Source finding context: Make one canonical authority path for rating conversion and overrides: define the authoritative mapping among the three grade scales in the ontology or a governed referenced artifact, specify precedence between engine-derived and manually adjusted grades, and require override reason, actor, timestamp/as_of, and audit status if report-grade adjustments remain allowed. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's declared purpose as the shared concept authority for risk engines and reporting systems. Source finding context: When the engine, reporting team, or another consumer maps rating scales differently, or when reporting manually adjusts `Exposure.risk_grade` without canonical precedence and audit semantics. Source finding context: The same borrower or exposure can carry different risk classifications across engine and reporting outputs, weakening trust in risk decisions and reports produced from the supposedly shared ontology. Source finding context: The ontology does not assign canonical authority, precedence, or governance for translating and overriding its core rating concepts. Source finding context: The target states that the ontology is intended to be the shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: The target contains three coexisting grade scales and says conversion is performed by each consuming system. Source finding context: The reporting team may manually adjust a grade that is otherwise said to be derived from engine output. Source finding context: These local conversions and adjustments can produce divergent risk classifications under a document whose purpose is shared authority. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / rating scale model Source finding context: materialized-input.md: Borrower.internal_rating note says "1~10 숫자 등급 체계"; RiskRating.grade enum values [R1, R2, R3, R4, R5] says mapping table is in the risk-team wiki; Exposure.risk_grade enum values [AAA, AA, A, BBB, BB, B, CCC]; notes say the three borrower grade scales coexist and each consumer system performs conversion. Source finding context: 등급 체계가 온톨로지 내부에서 버전 있는 변환 개념으로 모델링되지 않아 등급 체계 변경 시 소비 시스템별 재해석이 필요하다. Source finding context: evolution 관점에서 변경 내성의 핵심은 외부 표준이나 내부 등급 정책이 바뀌어도 기존 데이터와 규칙의 연속성이 유지되는지다. 현재 증거는 세 스케일의 병존과 소비자별 변환을 명시하므로, 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 같은 차주/익스포저를 다르게 매핑할 수 있고 새 스케일 도입 시 모든 소비 시스템의 변환 로직을 동시에 수정해야 한다. 이는 공유 개념 기준이라는 목적을 직접 약화한다. Source finding context: 등급 스케일을 독립 개념으로 승격하고 scale_id, grade_code, effective_from/effective_to, version, source_authority를 둔다. RiskRating.grade와 Exposure.risk_grade는 원천 등급과 보고 등급을 구분해 참조하게 하고, 변환은 온톨로지 안의 RatingMapping 엔티티 또는 규칙 세트로 버전 관리한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001 Source finding context: 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유할 여신 리스크 등급의 개념 기준과 변경 후 데이터 연속성. Source finding context: 등급 단계가 추가되거나 기존 R1~R5, 1~10, AAA~CCC 중 하나가 개편될 때 변환 권위가 온톨로지 밖의 위키와 소비 시스템별 구현으로 분산된다. Source finding context: 같은 원천 등급이 시스템마다 다른 보고 등급으로 해석될 수 있어 과거/현재 보고 비교와 리스크 엔진 산출물의 재현성이 약해진다. Source finding context: 등급 변환을 온톨로지의 버전 있는 권위 개념으로 닫지 않고 외부 위키 및 소비 시스템별 변환에 위임한다. Source finding context: 대상은 세 개의 등급 표현을 별도 속성으로 선언한다. Source finding context: RiskRating.grade의 Borrower.internal_rating 매핑은 온톨로지 내부가 아니라 리스크팀 위키를 참조한다. Source finding context: 세 스케일의 변환은 각 소비 시스템이 수행한다고 명시되어 공유 변환 권위가 분산된다. Source finding context: 등급 스케일 변경 시 기존 데이터와 새 데이터의 연속 비교를 온톨로지 자체로 보장할 수 없다.
  - affected purpose: The shared concept authority for risk-engine and reporting-system credit-risk grades, including continuity after rating scale changes. Source finding context: The ontology's declared purpose as the shared concept authority for risk engines and reporting systems. Source finding context: 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유할 여신 리스크 등급의 개념 기준과 변경 후 데이터 연속성.
  - failure condition: When grade scales are converted, manually adjusted, or changed, consumers rely on external wiki mappings or local implementations instead of an ontology-governed mapping. Source finding context: When the engine, reporting team, or another consumer maps rating scales differently, or when reporting manually adjusts `Exposure.risk_grade` without canonical precedence and audit semantics. Source finding context: 등급 단계가 추가되거나 기존 R1~R5, 1~10, AAA~CCC 중 하나가 개편될 때 변환 권위가 온톨로지 밖의 위키와 소비 시스템별 구현으로 분산된다.
  - impact: The same borrower or exposure can be classified differently across systems while still appearing compliant with the shared ontology, weakening risk decisions, reports, and historical comparability. Source finding context: The same borrower or exposure can carry different risk classifications across engine and reporting outputs, weakening trust in risk decisions and reports produced from the supposedly shared ontology. Source finding context: 같은 원천 등급이 시스템마다 다른 보고 등급으로 해석될 수 있어 과거/현재 보고 비교와 리스크 엔진 산출물의 재현성이 약해진다.
  - root hypothesis: Rating conversion is not modeled as a governed, versioned ontology concept, so grade meaning and continuity are delegated to external wiki or consumer implementations.
