  - source lenses: axiology, evolution
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - materiality: This is material because the ontology is meant to be the shared concept authority for risk engines and reporting systems. Rating grades are purpose-critical to that shared authority: if R1-R5, 1-10, and AAA-CCC conversions are left to local consumers or external wiki mappings, the same borrower or exposure can receive different classifications across engine output, reports, and historical comparisons.
  - root cause: The starting point is that rating conversion is not modeled as a governed, versioned ontology concept. Because the ontology does not own the mapping, source authority, effective period, precedence, or override semantics for grades, meaning and continuity are delegated to external wiki material and consumer implementations.
  - causal path: The causal path is supported by the shared-authority purpose, the presence of three grade scales, the note that each consuming system performs conversion, and the rule allowing reporting to manually adjust a grade derived from RiskRating.grade. Together these make local conversion and local override authority possible, which can produce divergent risk classifications under a document that is supposed to preserve common meaning.
  - action: Promote rating scales and mappings into governed, versioned ontology concepts. The fix should define authoritative mappings among the three scales, source authority, effective_from/effective_to or version fields, precedence between engine-derived and reporting-adjusted grades, and audit semantics for any allowed override, including reason, actor, timestamp/as_of, and audit status. This should be closed before the next stage because downstream reporting and engine behavior depend on stable shared grade meaning.

- issue-002 (high): Limit utilization is materially under-specified because the ontology defines a shared risk/reporting formula without making its calculation time, data freshness, FX basis, numerator, denominator, and CRM or external limit-source inputs authoritative parts of the contract.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, structure, semantics
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 6/6 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: axiology, coverage, logic, semantics, structure, evolution
  - remaining disagreement: 0/6 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: axiology, evolution
  - issue statement: The utilization formula relies on stale or system-specific inputs without making calculation time, freshness, FX authority, and CRM limit contribution part of the ontology contract. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / ExposureAggregate and Limit utilization rule Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `ExposureAggregate.total_amount` is stored from a month-end batch and intraday new loans are excluded until the next batch; `classification_rules[1]` defines limit utilization as `ExposureAggregate.total_amount / Limit.approved_amount`; `Limit.approved_amount` notes CRM available_limit is jointly managed. Source finding context: A stale batch aggregate is used as the authoritative numerator for limit utilization without temporal freshness boundaries. Source finding context: The declared purpose is a shared concept basis for risk engine and reporting. A limit utilization concept used for credit risk control is purpose-critical, but the model bakes in a known temporal lag without naming it as a stale snapshot, without defining when it is valid for decisions, and without separating reporting snapshot semantics from real-time risk-control semantics. This accepts a hidden tradeoff: operational convenience of batch storage over timely risk appetite and limit visibility. Source finding context: Define separate concepts for batch reporting exposure and decision-current exposure, require `as_of`/calculation timestamp and source freshness on both numerator and denominator, and constrain limit-utilization use by context, for example `reporting_utilization` versus `decision_utilization`. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-002 Source finding context: The ontology's purpose as a shared concept basis for risk engine and reporting, especially for limit utilization and risk exposure controls. Source finding context: When intraday lending or CRM limit changes occur after the month-end aggregate but before the next batch, and systems use the ontology's limit-utilization formula as if it represented current exposure. Source finding context: Risk engine or reports can understate utilization and risk appetite pressure, making the shared ontology operationally misleading for control decisions or cross-system reconciliation. Source finding context: The ontology does not separate reporting snapshots from decision-current exposure/limit semantics despite using the stale aggregate in a control formula. Source finding context: The target defines borrower total exposure as an aggregate of all exposure amounts. Source finding context: The stored aggregate is only calculated by month-end batch and omits intraday new lending until the next batch. Source finding context: The ontology uses that stored aggregate directly as the numerator for limit utilization. Source finding context: The denominator is also cross-system because `Limit.approved_amount` is jointly managed with CRM `available_limit`, but no common freshness or as-of rule is defined for the utilization calculation. Source finding context: A known-lag aggregate can therefore be interpreted as current utilization by the shared risk/reporting ontology. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / exposure aggregation and limit-utilization rule Source finding context: materialized-input.md: ExposureAggregate.total_amount note says monthly batch storage excludes intraday new loans until next batch; classification rule says currency conversion uses each system's same-day FX table; Limit.approved_amount note says CRM available_limit is jointly managed. Source finding context: 한도 소진율과 총익스포저 계산이 계산시점·환율권위·외부 한도 권위를 버전 있는 입력으로 고정하지 않아 신규 데이터 주기나 환율 정책 변경에 취약하다. Source finding context: 새로운 실시간 심사, 일중 리스크 모니터링, 다통화 포트폴리오 보고, 외부 CRM 교체 같은 변경이 들어오면 기존 Limit 소진율 의미가 그대로 확장되지 않는다. 같은 borrower와 amount라도 월말 배치/일중 값, 시스템별 환율 테이블, CRM 합산 방식에 따라 결과가 달라질 수 있어 보고 시스템과 리스크 엔진의 공유 기준이 변경에 따라 갈라진다. Source finding context: ExposureAggregate와 LimitUtilization을 계산 산출물로 모델링하고 calculation_as_of, data_cutoff_at, calculation_version, fx_rate_source, fx_rate_date, included_limit_sources를 속성 또는 참조로 둔다. CRM available_limit 같은 외부 기여분은 ExternalLimitComponent로 분리해 source_system과 effective interval을 명시한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002 Source finding context: 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유하는 한도 소진율, 총익스포저, 다통화 환산 기준. Source finding context: 일중 데이터 수용, 환율 테이블 변경, CRM 한도 소스 변경, 또는 다통화 보고 확장 시 계산 입력 권위와 기준시점이 시스템별로 달라진다. Source finding context: 한도 위반 여부나 소진율 보고가 계산 시점과 소비 시스템에 따라 달라져 변경 후에도 같은 개념을 유지한다는 신뢰가 낮아진다. Source finding context: 계산 산출물의 시간성, 환율 권위, 외부 한도 구성요소를 온톨로지 내부의 버전 있는 입력으로 모델링하지 않는다. Source finding context: ExposureAggregate.total_amount는 월말 배치 저장값이며 일중 신규 여신은 다음 배치까지 제외된다. Source finding context: Limit 소진율은 이 저장 집계값을 분자로 사용한다. Source finding context: 다통화 환산은 각 시스템의 당일 환율 테이블을 사용한다고 되어 있어 단일 환율 권위나 버전 식별자가 없다. Source finding context: 승인 한도는 CRM available_limit과 합산 관리된다고 되어 있지만 합산 구성요소와 기준시점이 개념화되어 있지 않다. Source finding context: 데이터 주기나 환율/CRM 소스가 변경되면 같은 Limit 소진율 개념의 연속성과 재현성이 깨질 수 있다.
  - affected purpose: The shared risk-engine and reporting basis for limit utilization, total exposure, and risk exposure controls. Source finding context: The ontology's purpose as a shared concept basis for risk engine and reporting, especially for limit utilization and risk exposure controls. Source finding context: 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유하는 한도 소진율, 총익스포저, 다통화 환산 기준.
  - failure condition: When intraday lending, FX policy, CRM limit sources, or reporting cycles differ, systems interpret the same utilization rule with different input timing or authority. Source finding context: When intraday lending or CRM limit changes occur after the month-end aggregate but before the next batch, and systems use the ontology's limit-utilization formula as if it represented current exposure. Source finding context: 일중 데이터 수용, 환율 테이블 변경, CRM 한도 소스 변경, 또는 다통화 보고 확장 시 계산 입력 권위와 기준시점이 시스템별로 달라진다.
  - impact: Limit utilization, breach pressure, and exposure reporting can diverge by system or calculation time, undermining trust in the shared risk-control concept. Source finding context: Risk engine or reports can understate utilization and risk appetite pressure, making the shared ontology operationally misleading for control decisions or cross-system reconciliation. Source finding context: 한도 위반 여부나 소진율 보고가 계산 시점과 소비 시스템에 따라 달라져 변경 후에도 같은 개념을 유지한다는 신뢰가 낮아진다.
  - root hypothesis: Limit-utilization is modeled without versioned calculation inputs for data cutoff, calculation time, FX source, and external limit source authority.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `ExposureAggregate.definition` says it is total borrower exposure and all `Exposure.amount` sum.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `ExposureAggregate.total_amount` note says it is calculated by month-end batch and intraday new lending is not reflected until the next batch.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `classification_rules[1]` defines utilization as `ExposureAggregate.total_amount / Limit.approved_amount`.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Limit.approved_amount` note says CRM `available_limit` is jointly managed.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `ExposureAggregate.total_amount` is stored from a month-end batch and intraday new loans are excluded until the next batch; `classification_rules[1]` defines limit utilization as `ExposureAggregate.total_amount / Limit.approved_amount`; `Limit.approved_amount` notes CRM available_limit is jointly managed.`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002`, `materialized-input.md: ExposureAggregate.total_amount note`, `materialized-input.md: classification_rules Limit 소진율 rule`, `materialized-input.md: Limit.approved_amount note`, `materialized-input.md: ExposureAggregate.total_amount note says monthly batch storage excludes intraday new loans until next batch; classification rule says currency conversion uses each system's same-day FX table; Limit.approved_amount note says CRM available_limit is jointly managed.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-002`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-010`, `rel-006`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-006`, `finding-002.cause-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `ExposureAggregate.definition` says borrower-level total exposure is the sum of all `Exposure.amount`.`, `finding-002.cause-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `ExposureAggregate.total_amount` note.`, `finding-002.cause-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `classification_rules[1]`.`, `finding-002.cause-004`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Limit.approved_amount` note.`, `finding-002.cause-005`, `Embedded Materialized Input: shared-authority header comment.`, `finding-010.cause-001`, `finding-010.cause-002`, `finding-010.cause-003`, `finding-010.cause-004`, `finding-010.cause-005`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-002`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.logic`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-002`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-002`, `rel-007`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-003`, `rel-008`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: axiology, evolution
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens the declared purpose of a shared risk-engine and reporting basis: the same utilization rule can be read as a month-end reporting snapshot, an intraday control value, or a system-local calculation depending on which exposure aggregate, FX table, and limit-source state a consumer uses. That can make utilization, breach pressure, and exposure reporting diverge across systems while appearing to use the same ontology concept.
  - root cause: The starting point is that limit utilization is not modeled as a versioned calculation output with explicit input authority. Because calculation_as_of, data_cutoff_at, calculation version, FX source/date, and included CRM or external limit-source components are absent, consumers must infer those dimensions locally before the formula can have a stable meaning.
  - causal path: The causal path is: borrower total exposure is defined as an aggregate of exposure amounts; the stored ExposureAggregate.total_amount is a month-end batch value that omits intraday new lending until the next batch; the utilization rule uses that stored aggregate directly as the numerator; the denominator also depends on Limit.approved_amount jointly managed with CRM available_limit; and FX conversion may use each system's same-day table. Without a shared as-of, cutoff, FX, and denominator authority, a known-lag aggregate can be interpreted as current utilization and produce different shared risk-control values.
  - action: Model ExposureAggregate and LimitUtilization as calculation outputs, not just stored values or prose-backed formulas. Add authoritative calculation_as_of, data_cutoff_at, calculation_version, fx_rate_source, fx_rate_date, and included_limit_sources; represent CRM or other external limit contributions as explicit components with source system and effective interval. This should be fixed before the next stage because later reporting, risk-engine, CRM, and FX extensions depend on the same utilization contract.

- issue-004 (high): Manual Exposure.risk_grade overrides are a material issue because the ontology permits reporting-side grade changes but does not model how a reported grade resolves from an engine-derived value versus an approved override, nor does it preserve the evidence needed to audit that override.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, structure, logic, semantics
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 6/6 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - remaining disagreement: 0/6 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: coverage
  - issue statement: Manual risk-grade overrides are authorized but lack audit evidence concepts for actor, time, reason, approval, and source-versus-adjusted values. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / classification_rules: Exposure.risk_grade manual adjustment Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: classification_rules[0] says Exposure.risk_grade is derived from RiskRating.grade but the reporting team may manually adjust it based on market conditions; Exposure.risk_grade has only the adjusted enum value and no adjustment evidence attributes or entity. Source finding context: Manual risk-grade overrides are in scope but the ontology lacks audit evidence concepts for the override action. Source finding context: A shared concept authority for a risk engine and reporting system must let both systems distinguish engine-derived grades from manually adjusted reported grades. The target explicitly allows manual adjustment, while the only modeled grade-bearing attributes are Borrower.internal_rating, RiskRating.grade, and Exposure.risk_grade. None of the entities or relations capture an override action, actor, timestamp, rationale, approval, or prior/current grade pair, so the reporting value cannot be reconstructed or audited from the ontology itself. Source finding context: Add a bounded override/audit concept such as RiskGradeAdjustment with exposure_ref, source_rating_ref or source_grade, adjusted_grade, actor, adjusted_at, reason/market_basis, approval_ref/status, and effective period; relate Exposure.risk_grade to the latest effective approved adjustment or the derived RiskRating mapping when no adjustment exists. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology is intended as a shared concept authority for the risk engine and reporting system. Source finding context: When Exposure.risk_grade is manually changed by the reporting team, downstream users cannot tell whether a reported grade is engine-derived or adjusted, nor reconstruct the actor, time, and basis for the reported value. Source finding context: This weakens auditability and cross-system trust for a core risk classification value, especially because the target itself authorizes manual changes to that value. Source finding context: The ontology models adjusted risk grade as a bare Exposure attribute while placing the manual adjustment rule only in free-text classification_rules. Source finding context: Exposure.risk_grade is represented as a single enum attribute on Exposure. Source finding context: The classification rule permits the reporting team to manually adjust that value based on market conditions. Source finding context: No entity or attribute captures the manual adjustment action, actor, timestamp, basis, approval, or source-versus-adjusted value.
  - affected purpose: The shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting classifications. Source finding context: The ontology is intended as a shared concept authority for the risk engine and reporting system.
  - failure condition: When reporting manually changes Exposure.risk_grade, downstream users cannot reconstruct whether, when, why, or by whom the value was adjusted. Source finding context: When Exposure.risk_grade is manually changed by the reporting team, downstream users cannot tell whether a reported grade is engine-derived or adjusted, nor reconstruct the actor, time, and basis for the reported value.
  - impact: Auditability and cross-system trust weaken for a core risk classification value that the ontology itself allows reporting users to change. Source finding context: This weakens auditability and cross-system trust for a core risk classification value, especially because the target itself authorizes manual changes to that value.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology models adjusted risk grade as a bare Exposure attribute while placing the manual adjustment rule only in free-text classification_rules.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: header comment states the ontology is a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Exposure.risk_grade note says it is used by the reporting team.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: classification_rules[0] permits reporting-team manual adjustment of Exposure.risk_grade.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities list contains no adjustment/audit/approval evidence entity or attributes for risk-grade overrides.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: classification_rules[0] says Exposure.risk_grade is derived from RiskRating.grade but the reporting team may manually adjust it based on market conditions; Exposure.risk_grade has only the adjusted enum value and no adjustment evidence attributes or entity.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-004`, `finding-004.cause-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Exposure.attributes.risk_grade values [AAA, AA, A, BBB, BB, B, CCC].`, `finding-004.cause-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: classification_rules[0].`, `finding-004.cause-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities Borrower, Exposure, Collateral, RiskRating, Limit, ExposureAggregate, RiskAppetite and their listed attributes.`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.logic`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-004`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: coverage
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: The ontology is meant to be a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting classifications. When a core classification value can be manually changed without actor, time, reason, approval, source grade, adjusted grade, and effective/resolution evidence, downstream users cannot trust or reconstruct whether the reported grade came from the engine or from reporting judgment.
  - root cause: The starting point is that Exposure.risk_grade is modeled as a single adjusted enum attribute while the manual-adjustment permission exists only in classification rule text. That leaves no authoritative concept for derived grade state, adjusted grade state, override precedence, or audit evidence.
  - causal path: Exposure.risk_grade holds only one grade value. The classification rule says the value is derived from RiskRating.grade but may be manually adjusted by the reporting team based on market conditions. Because the entity model has no adjustment event, actor, timestamp, rationale, approval, or source-versus-adjusted value pair, the ontology cannot distinguish normal derivation from override or support audit traversal after the reported value changes.
  - action: Add a bounded RiskGradeAdjustment or equivalent value-resolution concept before the next stage. It should connect the exposure to the source RiskRating or source grade, adjusted grade, actor, adjusted_at time, reason or market basis, approval reference/status, and effective period, and define that Exposure.risk_grade resolves to the latest effective approved adjustment when present or to the derived RiskRating mapping otherwise.

- issue-005 (high): Time-dependent ratings, limits, mappings, and exchange-rate inputs are modeled as current or undated values, so the ontology cannot reliably reconstruct the values that were valid for a historical report or risk-engine decision.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: coverage
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: coverage
  - issue statement: Time-dependent ratings, limits, mappings, and exchange rates lack validity periods or history, preventing point-in-time reconstruction. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / rating and exchange-rate dependent values Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: RiskRating.grade has no effective date; Borrower.internal_rating has no time fields; Exposure.risk_grade has no effective date; classification_rules[1] uses each system's same-day exchange-rate table, but no exchange-rate concept exists. Source finding context: Time-dependent classification inputs are modeled without validity periods or history, preventing point-in-time reconstruction. Source finding context: The review target is a shared concept basis for risk calculation and reporting. For historical reports or replayed risk-engine decisions, both systems need the value that was valid at the relevant point in time. The target explicitly mentions monthly batch aggregate timing and same-day exchange-rate tables, but the ontology lacks effective_from/effective_to, as_of, version, or source-table references for several time-dependent inputs, so historical utilization and grade outputs cannot be reconstructed from the concept model. Source finding context: Introduce temporal validity consistently for time-dependent concepts: add effective_from/effective_to or as_of/version fields to RiskRating, Borrower rating, Exposure.risk_grade, Limit.approved_amount, and any mapping table; add an ExchangeRate or CurrencyConversionRate concept with source_system/table authority, currency pair, rate_date/time, and rate value. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002 Source finding context: The ontology is meant to support shared risk-engine and reporting interpretation of credit-risk values. Source finding context: For a prior reporting date or risk decision, the systems need to reconstruct the rating, limit, mapping, and exchange-rate values valid at that time, but the ontology only stores current or undated values for several inputs. Source finding context: This can make historical reports non-reproducible and risk calculations inconsistent across systems because point-in-time authority is absent for core classification inputs. Source finding context: Temporal coverage is applied only selectively rather than as a concept dimension for all point-in-time risk inputs. Source finding context: Some concepts include point-in-time markers, such as Collateral.appraised_at and ExposureAggregate.as_of. Source finding context: Other time-dependent values, including ratings, risk grades, approved limits, and exchange rates, lack equivalent validity/history fields or entities. Source finding context: Because classification and utilization calculations depend on those values, historical outputs cannot be reconstructed from the ontology alone.
  - affected purpose: Shared risk-engine and reporting interpretation of historical and current credit-risk values. Source finding context: The ontology is meant to support shared risk-engine and reporting interpretation of credit-risk values.
  - failure condition: For a prior reporting date or risk decision, systems need the rating, limit, mapping, or FX value valid at that time, but the ontology only provides current or undated values. Source finding context: For a prior reporting date or risk decision, the systems need to reconstruct the rating, limit, mapping, and exchange-rate values valid at that time, but the ontology only stores current or undated values for several inputs.
  - impact: Historical reports and risk calculations can become non-reproducible or inconsistent because point-in-time authority is absent for core inputs. Source finding context: This can make historical reports non-reproducible and risk calculations inconsistent across systems because point-in-time authority is absent for core classification inputs.
  - root hypothesis: Temporal coverage is applied only selectively rather than as a concept dimension for all point-in-time risk inputs.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Borrower.internal_rating has no validity or as_of attributes.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: RiskRating.grade and score have no validity or as_of attributes.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Exposure.risk_grade and ltv lack validity fields; ltv note says it is entered at review time.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Limit.approved_amount has no approval/effective date.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: classification_rules[1] says currency conversion uses each system's same-day exchange-rate table, but no exchange-rate entity is defined.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: ExposureAggregate.total_amount has as_of and a monthly batch note, showing at least one temporal value is recognized.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: RiskRating.grade has no effective date; Borrower.internal_rating has no time fields; Exposure.risk_grade has no effective date; classification_rules[1] uses each system's same-day exchange-rate table, but no exchange-rate concept exists.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-005`, `finding-005.cause-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Collateral.attributes.appraised_at.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: ExposureAggregate.attributes.as_of.`, `finding-005.cause-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Borrower.internal_rating, RiskRating.grade/score, Exposure.risk_grade, Limit.approved_amount, classification_rules[1].`, `finding-005.cause-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: classification_rules[0] and classification_rules[1].`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.logic`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-005`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-005`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-003`, `rel-008`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-005`, `rel-012`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: coverage
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens the declared purpose of shared risk-engine and reporting interpretation because historical and current credit-risk values require point-in-time authority. If ratings, approved limits, risk grades, mappings, and FX rates are not temporally qualified, two systems can replay the same reporting date or decision using different implicit values, making reports non-reproducible and calculations inconsistent.
  - root cause: The root cause is selective temporal modeling: the ontology recognizes time on some concepts, such as aggregate as-of dates and collateral appraisal timestamps, but does not apply validity, history, version, or source timing as a consistent dimension across all point-in-time risk inputs. That selective coverage is the earliest structural gap from which the reconstruction failure follows.
  - causal path: The causal path starts with existing temporal markers on only some concepts, then shows that other changing inputs such as Borrower.internal_rating, RiskRating grade and score, Exposure.risk_grade, Limit.approved_amount, and exchange-rate-dependent conversion lack equivalent effective, as-of, version, or authority fields. Because classification and utilization calculations depend on those inputs, the ontology cannot identify which value was valid at the relevant prior date, so historical outputs cannot be reconstructed from the model alone.
  - action: Add temporal validity semantics consistently to all time-dependent risk inputs, using effective_from/effective_to, as_of, version, or equivalent history fields where appropriate. Also introduce an authoritative ExchangeRate or CurrencyConversionRate concept that records source system or table authority, currency pair, rate date or time, and rate value. This should be fixed before the next stage because downstream classification, utilization, reporting, and replay logic depend on these values having point-in-time authority.

- issue-006 (high): Values shared across the risk engine and reporting systems are not governed by a single ontology authority. Grade mappings, limit values, and exchange-rate sources are delegated to external or per-system sources, so consumers must reconcile them independently.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none

  - issue statement: Values shared across systems lack a single authority concept, leaving consumers to reconcile grade mappings, limits, and FX sources themselves. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / multi-system managed values and grade scales Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: RiskRating.grade note sends mapping to a risk-team wiki; Limit.approved_amount note says approval system records it and CRM available_limit is managed together; classification_rules[1] uses each system's exchange-rate table; notes say each consuming system performs grade-scale conversion. Source finding context: Values explicitly shared across systems lack a single authority concept, leaving consumers to reconcile competing sources themselves. Source finding context: Coverage of single authority is required when values are managed across systems. The target's declared purpose is to be a shared concept authority, but several fields are explicitly delegated to external or per-consumer sources without an authoritative entity, relation, or precedence rule. That omission means the risk engine and reporting system can legally produce different grade mappings, limit utilization, or currency-converted values while still conforming to the ontology text. Source finding context: Add authority-bearing concepts or attributes for each parallel value: a canonical RatingScaleMapping with owner/version/effective period; a LimitAuthority or LimitSnapshot separating approved_amount from CRM available_limit with source_system and precedence; and a governed ExchangeRateSource/ExchangeRate table. State which source controls each calculation and how conflicts are resolved. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-003 Source finding context: The artifact declares itself the shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When systems disagree on grade conversion, available limit, or exchange rate, the ontology provides no authoritative source or precedence rule to resolve the discrepancy. Source finding context: The shared ontology fails its authority purpose for central risk/reporting calculations and allows inconsistent but ontology-compliant outputs across consuming systems. Source finding context: The ontology acknowledges cross-system coexistence but does not promote mapping, limit availability, or exchange-rate authority into governed ontology concepts. Source finding context: The target declares multiple system-specific sources for grade conversion, limits, and exchange rates. Source finding context: No entity or relation names a canonical source, precedence rule, owner, version, or conflict-resolution concept for those values. Source finding context: Therefore the risk engine and reporting system can each use different external mappings or rates while remaining inside the documented ontology.
  - affected purpose: The artifact's declared role as shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: The artifact declares itself the shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems.
  - failure condition: When systems disagree on grade conversion, available limit, or exchange rate, the ontology provides no canonical source or precedence rule to resolve the discrepancy. Source finding context: When systems disagree on grade conversion, available limit, or exchange rate, the ontology provides no authoritative source or precedence rule to resolve the discrepancy.
  - impact: Central calculations can diverge while remaining ontology-compliant, so the shared ontology fails its authority purpose. Source finding context: The shared ontology fails its authority purpose for central risk/reporting calculations and allows inconsistent but ontology-compliant outputs across consuming systems.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology acknowledges cross-system coexistence but does not promote mapping, limit availability, or exchange-rate authority into governed ontology concepts.

  - action candidates: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - materiality: This is material because the artifact declares itself the shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. If systems disagree on grade conversion, available limit, or exchange rates, the ontology provides no canonical source or precedence rule to resolve the discrepancy. As a result, central calculations can diverge while still remaining ontology-compliant.
  - root cause: The root cause is that the ontology acknowledges cross-system coexistence but does not promote rating mappings, limit availability, or exchange-rate authority into governed ontology concepts. That is the starting point because the problem is not merely missing documentation on individual fields; the model lacks authority-bearing concepts with owner, version, effective period, source, precedence, and conflict-resolution semantics.
  - causal path: The causal path starts with the ontology declaring multiple system-specific or external sources for grade conversion, limits, and exchange rates. It then leaves those values without an entity or relation naming a canonical source, precedence rule, owner, version, or conflict-resolution mechanism. Therefore the risk engine and reporting system can each use different external mappings or rates while still conforming to the documented ontology.
  - action: Add authority-bearing concepts for each shared value class: a canonical RatingScaleMapping with owner, version, and effective period; a LimitAuthority or LimitSnapshot that separates approved_amount from CRM available_limit and records source_system plus precedence; and a governed ExchangeRateSource or ExchangeRate table. The ontology should state which source controls each calculation and how conflicts are resolved before the next stage, because this issue is framed as a next-step blocker that must close before release-quality use.

- issue-012 (high): Collateral is wrongly modeled as a subtype of Exposure. The ontology should treat Collateral as a separate securing-asset entity and connect it to Exposure through the existing secured_by relationship instead of inheritance.
  - issue stance agreement: 4/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: coverage, evolution
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 2/2 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: semantics, logic
  - remaining disagreement: 0/2 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: semantics
  - issue statement: Collateral is semantically misclassified as an Exposure subtype, creating a foundational type boundary error. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml entities.Collateral Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Collateral.definition = "익스포저를 담보하는 자산. 담보 평가의 기본 단위." and Collateral.is_a = Exposure Source finding context: Collateral is semantically misclassified as an Exposure subtype. Source finding context: The embedded target defines Exposure as credit exposure to a borrower, while Collateral is defined as an asset that secures an exposure. These are different ontological types: a credit obligation/position versus a securing asset. Because the artifact is intended as a shared concept authority for risk engines and reporting systems, this subtype assertion can cause consumers to inherit exposure semantics for collateral records and misinterpret collateral assets as exposure instances. Source finding context: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure`; keep Collateral as a separate entity and express the relationship through `Exposure secured_by Collateral` or an equivalent association with explicit cardinality/role. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's declared purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems covering exposures, grades, limits, and collateral. Source finding context: When a consuming engine or report treats subtype assertions as inheritance or classification authority, Collateral becomes an Exposure instance despite the definitions distinguishing collateral assets from credit exposures. Source finding context: This weakens trust in the shared taxonomy because a foundational entity boundary is semantically wrong, allowing collateral records to be counted, filtered, validated, or reported under exposure semantics. Source finding context: The ontology uses subtype inheritance for Collateral even though its own definitions only support an associative securing relationship to Exposure. Source finding context: Collateral is asserted as a subtype of Exposure via `is_a: Exposure`. Source finding context: The definitions distinguish Exposure as a credit exposure to a borrower and Collateral as an asset that secures an exposure. Source finding context: The same model already has an associative relation `Exposure secured_by Collateral`, so the subtype assertion is not needed to express the securing relationship.
  - affected purpose: The shared concept authority covering exposures, grades, limits, and collateral for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: The ontology's declared purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems covering exposures, grades, limits, and collateral.
  - failure condition: When consumers treat subtype assertions as classification authority, Collateral becomes an Exposure instance despite definitions distinguishing collateral assets from credit exposures. Source finding context: When a consuming engine or report treats subtype assertions as inheritance or classification authority, Collateral becomes an Exposure instance despite the definitions distinguishing collateral assets from credit exposures.
  - impact: Collateral records may be counted, filtered, validated, or reported under exposure semantics, weakening trust in the shared taxonomy. Source finding context: This weakens trust in the shared taxonomy because a foundational entity boundary is semantically wrong, allowing collateral records to be counted, filtered, validated, or reported under exposure semantics.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology uses subtype inheritance for Collateral even though its own definitions only support an associative securing relationship to Exposure.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: header declares shared concept authority purpose`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Exposure.definition`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Collateral.definition`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Collateral.is_a = Exposure`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Collateral.definition = "익스포저를 담보하는 자산. 담보 평가의 기본 단위." and Collateral.is_a = Exposure`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-014`, `finding-014.cause-001`, `finding-014.cause-002`, `finding-014.cause-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: relations entry from Exposure to Collateral kind secured_by`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-012`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.logic`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-012`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-010`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: fix_now
  - materiality: This is material because the ontology is meant to be the shared concept authority for risk engines and reporting systems covering exposures, grades, limits, and collateral. If Collateral inherits Exposure semantics, downstream consumers may count, filter, validate, or report collateral assets as credit exposures, undermining trust in a foundational taxonomy boundary.
  - root cause: The root cause is the subtype assertion `Collateral.is_a: Exposure`. The ontology's own definitions distinguish Exposure as a borrower credit exposure and Collateral as an asset securing an exposure, so inheritance is the first incorrect modeling decision that creates the downstream confusion.
  - causal path: The causal path starts with Collateral being asserted as an Exposure subtype. The definitions then show that the two concepts belong to different ontological types: credit obligation or position versus securing asset. Because the model already includes `Exposure secured_by Collateral`, the intended relationship can be represented associatively, making the inheritance edge both unnecessary and misleading.
  - action: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure`, keep Collateral as an independent securing-asset entity, and retain or clarify the explicit `Exposure secured_by Collateral` association with appropriate role and cardinality. This fixes the type boundary at the source while preserving the valid business relationship between exposures and collateral.

- issue-013 (high): Three credit-grade concepts overlap semantically but the ontology does not define an authoritative internal mapping among them. As a result, Borrower.internal_rating, RiskRating.grade, and Exposure.risk_grade can appear to participate in one shared credit-risk taxonomy while carrying different meanings across engine and reporting consumers.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: semantics
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: semantics
  - issue statement: Three credit-grade concepts overlap semantically but lack an authoritative ontology-internal mapping. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml rating concepts Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Borrower.internal_rating values are described as 1~10; RiskRating.grade values are [R1, R2, R3, R4, R5]; Exposure.risk_grade values are [AAA, AA, A, BBB, BB, B, CCC]; notes say the three scales coexist and each consuming system performs conversion. Source finding context: Three credit-grade concepts are semantically overlapping but lack an authoritative mapping inside the ontology. Source finding context: All three fields name a borrower/exposure credit risk grade, but their scale names and authorities differ. The target itself says this artifact is meant to be the shared concept authority between a risk engine and reporting systems; leaving semantically overlapping grades to separate consumer-side mappings means the same borrower or exposure risk meaning can diverge by system. Source finding context: Define canonical grade concepts and authority boundaries in the ontology: either model one canonical rating with explicit scale mappings/versioning, or split the concepts by meaning such as borrower internal rating, engine borrower rating, and report exposure grade with authoritative mapping rules and allowed override semantics. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002 Source finding context: Shared concept authority for the risk engine and reporting system, especially for ratings used in classification and reports. Source finding context: When the engine emits RiskRating.grade and reporting consumes Exposure.risk_grade or Borrower.internal_rating, each consumer may apply a different conversion or manual override without an ontology-governed semantic mapping. Source finding context: The ontology cannot reliably establish whether two systems are referring to the same risk grade meaning, so risk classification and reporting can disagree while still appearing compliant with the shared taxonomy. Source finding context: The ontology defines multiple overlapping rating scales but delegates conversion authority to external wiki/consumer systems instead of making the semantic mapping part of the shared concept model. Source finding context: Borrower.internal_rating, RiskRating.grade, and Exposure.risk_grade all represent credit or risk grade concepts with different scales. Source finding context: The classification rule derives Exposure.risk_grade from RiskRating.grade but also allows report-team manual adjustment based on market conditions. Source finding context: The notes state that conversion among the three coexisting scales is performed by each consuming system.
  - affected purpose: Shared concept authority for ratings used in risk classification and reporting. Source finding context: Shared concept authority for the risk engine and reporting system, especially for ratings used in classification and reports.
  - failure condition: When engine and reporting consumers translate among Borrower.internal_rating, RiskRating.grade, and Exposure.risk_grade, each may apply different conversion or manual override semantics. Source finding context: When the engine emits RiskRating.grade and reporting consumes Exposure.risk_grade or Borrower.internal_rating, each consumer may apply a different conversion or manual override without an ontology-governed semantic mapping.
  - impact: The ontology cannot prove whether systems are referring to the same risk-grade meaning, allowing classification disagreement under an apparently shared taxonomy. Source finding context: The ontology cannot reliably establish whether two systems are referring to the same risk grade meaning, so risk classification and reporting can disagree while still appearing compliant with the shared taxonomy.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology defines multiple overlapping rating scales but delegates conversion authority to external wiki/consumer systems instead of making the semantic mapping part of the shared concept model.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Borrower.internal_rating note`, `Embedded Materialized Input: RiskRating.grade note`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Exposure.risk_grade note`, `Embedded Materialized Input: classification_rules first rule`, `Embedded Materialized Input: notes first item`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Borrower.internal_rating values are described as 1~10; RiskRating.grade values are [R1, R2, R3, R4, R5]; Exposure.risk_grade values are [AAA, AA, A, BBB, BB, B, CCC]; notes say the three scales coexist and each consuming system performs conversion.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-015`, `finding-015.cause-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: RiskRating.definition and RiskRating.grade values`, `finding-015.cause-002`, `finding-015.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-013`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.logic`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-013`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-005`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-001`, `rel-003`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - materiality: This is material because the declared purpose is shared concept authority for ratings used in risk classification and reporting. If the ontology leaves grade conversion and override semantics to each consuming system, it cannot guarantee that the risk engine and reporting outputs refer to the same credit-grade meaning, so classification and reports may disagree while still looking compliant with the taxonomy.
  - root cause: The root cause is that the ontology defines multiple overlapping rating scales but delegates conversion authority to external wiki or consumer-side logic instead of making the mapping part of the shared concept model. That starts the problem because the semantic authority is absent before any downstream system translates, derives, or overrides a grade.
  - causal path: The causal path is supported by the three coexisting grade concepts: Borrower.internal_rating uses a 1-10 style scale, RiskRating.grade uses R1-R5, and Exposure.risk_grade uses AAA through CCC. The classification rule derives Exposure.risk_grade from RiskRating.grade and also allows report-team manual adjustment based on market conditions. The notes then state that conversions among these coexisting scales are performed by each consuming system, which makes divergent mappings and override meanings possible without violating any ontology-internal rule.
  - action: The required action is to define canonical grade concepts and authority boundaries in the ontology before the next stage. The fix can either model one canonical rating with explicit scale mappings and versioning, or split borrower internal rating, engine borrower rating, and report exposure grade as distinct concepts with governed mapping rules and allowed override semantics. This must happen before release because downstream classification and reporting depend on the same rating meanings being comparable and auditable.

- issue-008 (medium): Stateful credit-risk objects are modeled without lifecycle, terminal, remediation, archival, or post-close correction concepts, so the ontology cannot consistently represent whether exposures, limits, collateral, or risk-appetite compliance records are active, closed, expired, released, remediated, or corrected.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, semantics, structure, logic
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: coverage
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: coverage
  - issue statement: Stateful credit-risk objects lack lifecycle coverage for closure, expiry, release, remediation, archival, or post-close correction. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / lifecycle coverage for limits, exposures, collateral, and risk appetite Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: entities define Limit.approved_amount, Exposure.amount/product_type, Collateral.appraised_value/appraised_at, RiskAppetite.compliance_status, but no lifecycle states or termination/archive/disposal fields. Source finding context: Stateful credit-risk objects lack lifecycle coverage from creation through closure, expiry, archival, or post-close correction. Source finding context: Lifecycle coverage matters for shared risk/reporting concepts because calculations and reports often distinguish active exposure from closed exposure, approved limit from expired limit, pledged collateral from released collateral, and current breach from remediated breach. The target has a compliance_status enum for RiskAppetite and an appraised_at timestamp for Collateral, but no lifecycle model for the objects whose states affect inclusion, aging, and correction behavior. Source finding context: Add lifecycle/status attributes or separate lifecycle event concepts for Exposure, Limit, Collateral, and RiskAppetiteCompliance, including effective timestamps, terminal states, and correction/reissue events after a reporting period closes. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-005 Source finding context: The ontology is meant to provide shared concepts for risk engine calculations and reporting outputs. Source finding context: When an exposure closes, a limit expires, collateral is released/revalued, or a breach is remediated/corrected after reporting close, the ontology has no concept to represent the state transition or terminal condition. Source finding context: Risk engines and reports may include or exclude objects inconsistently because the shared taxonomy does not cover lifecycle state or post-terminal corrections. Source finding context: The ontology represents risk objects as current-value records instead of lifecycle-bearing records or event histories. Source finding context: The entity attributes emphasize current values and identifiers for Exposure, Limit, Collateral, and RiskAppetite. Source finding context: Lifecycle states and lifecycle events are absent for those entities, except for the narrow compliant/breached status on RiskAppetite. Source finding context: This absence prevents consistent representation of active versus terminal, remediated, released, or corrected records.
  - affected purpose: Shared concepts for risk-engine calculations and reporting outputs. Source finding context: The ontology is meant to provide shared concepts for risk engine calculations and reporting outputs.
  - failure condition: When exposures close, limits expire, collateral is released or revalued, or breaches are remediated after reporting close, no ontology concept represents the transition or terminal state. Source finding context: When an exposure closes, a limit expires, collateral is released/revalued, or a breach is remediated/corrected after reporting close, the ontology has no concept to represent the state transition or terminal condition.
  - impact: Risk engines and reports may include, exclude, or correct objects inconsistently because lifecycle state is not part of the shared taxonomy. Source finding context: Risk engines and reports may include or exclude objects inconsistently because the shared taxonomy does not cover lifecycle state or post-terminal corrections.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology represents risk objects as current-value records instead of lifecycle-bearing records or event histories.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-005`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Exposure attributes contain identity, borrower, amount, product_type, risk_grade, and ltv but no status/lifecycle fields.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Limit attributes contain approved_amount and currency but no approval date, expiry, active/closed status, or lifecycle event.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Collateral attributes contain appraised_value and appraised_at but no pledged/released/revalued lifecycle status.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: RiskAppetite.compliance_status has compliant/breached only, with no remediation, closure, or correction states.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entities define Limit.approved_amount, Exposure.amount/product_type, Collateral.appraised_value/appraised_at, RiskAppetite.compliance_status, but no lifecycle states or termination/archive/disposal fields.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-008`, `finding-008.cause-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: entity attribute lists.`, `finding-008.cause-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: RiskAppetite.compliance_status values [compliant, breached]; no other lifecycle statuses are listed.`, `finding-008.cause-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: complete entity and relation coverage.`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.logic`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-008`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-008`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-005`, `rel-012`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: coverage
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: This weakens the ontology's purpose as shared concepts for risk-engine calculations and reporting outputs because those consumers need common inclusion, exclusion, aging, and correction semantics. Without lifecycle state in the shared taxonomy, one report may treat a closed exposure, expired limit, released collateral item, or remediated breach differently from another even when they are describing the same risk object.
  - root cause: The starting point is the current-value modeling pattern: Exposure, Limit, Collateral, and RiskAppetite emphasize identifiers and present values such as amount, approved amount, appraised value, appraised timestamp, or compliant/breached status. Because the model does not make these objects lifecycle-bearing records or event histories, later closure, expiry, release, remediation, archive, and correction states have no authoritative place to attach.
  - causal path: The causal path is that the entity attributes first center on current values and identifiers; then lifecycle states and lifecycle events are absent, except for the narrow RiskAppetite compliant/breached enum; therefore consumers cannot express active versus terminal, remediated, released, revalued, archived, or corrected records consistently. The logic lens narrowed the defect to under-specified inclusion/exclusion predicates, but that refinement remains compatible with the broader lifecycle coverage gap accepted by the other lenses.
  - action: Add lifecycle/status attributes or lifecycle event concepts for Exposure, Limit, Collateral, and RiskAppetiteCompliance, including effective timestamps, terminal states, and post-period correction or reissue events. The action should establish shared state semantics before downstream risk-engine and reporting rules depend on these objects, because those rules need a common authority for whether a record is current, terminal, remediated, released, or corrected.

- issue-009 (medium): Core product, collateral, borrower segment, and grade classifications are modeled as closed enum literals, so ordinary business classification changes require ontology schema changes instead of governed code-set updates.
