  - impact: Classification change becomes a schema and consumer deployment problem rather than governed data/code-set management, increasing compatibility risk. Source finding context: 분류 변경이 데이터/코드셋 관리가 아니라 스키마 변경과 소비자 배포 문제가 되어 변경 비용과 호환성 위험이 커진다.
  - root hypothesis: Changeable business classification values are modeled as closed enum literals instead of governed, versioned code-set concepts.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-003`, `materialized-input.md: Borrower.segment enum`, `materialized-input.md: Exposure.product_type enum`, `materialized-input.md: Collateral.collateral_type enum`, `materialized-input.md: RiskRating.grade enum`, `materialized-input.md: Exposure.risk_grade enum`, `materialized-input.md: Borrower.segment enum values [retail, sme, corporate]; Exposure.product_type enum values [term_loan, revolving, guarantee]; Collateral.collateral_type enum values [real_estate, deposit, securities]; RiskRating.grade and Exposure.risk_grade also use closed value lists.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-011`, `finding-011.cause-001`, `finding-011.cause-002`, `materialized-input.md: entities section`, `materialized-input.md: relations section`, `finding-011.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-009`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.logic`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-009`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-006`, `rel-013`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: evolution
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: This weakens the ontology's extensibility and its role as a shared classification basis for risk engines and reporting systems. When new or retired categories require schema edits and coordinated consumer deployment, classification governance becomes more costly and compatibility risk rises.
  - root cause: The causal chain starts with changeable business classification values being embedded directly as closed attribute enums rather than represented as governed, versioned code-set concepts. That modeling choice removes stable code identity, lifecycle metadata, and independent change management from the classification layer.
  - causal path: The evidence path shows fixed enum lists on key classification properties such as borrower segment, exposure product type, collateral type, risk rating grade, and exposure risk grade. Because no separate classification entities are modeled for status, effective periods, hierarchy, or source authority, adding or retiring categories means changing the property value lists themselves, which makes a business taxonomy update a schema change.
  - action: Create versioned code-set entities such as ProductType, CollateralType, BorrowerSegment, and GradeCode with code, label, definition, hierarchy, status, effective_from/effective_to, and source_authority, then have attributes reference those code sets instead of enum literals. This should be handled as follow-up ontology modeling work because the code-set identity and lifecycle model must exist before dependent attributes can safely migrate to references.

- issue-010 (medium): Collateral is incorrectly modeled as an Exposure subtype even though the ontology defines it as a securing asset and already relates Exposure to Collateral through secured_by. This creates a material type-boundary contradiction that should be fixed before the ontology is used as a shared authority.
  - issue stance agreement: 4/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: coverage, evolution
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 2/2 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: logic, semantics
  - remaining disagreement: 0/2 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: logic
  - issue statement: Collateral is declared as an Exposure subtype even though the ontology defines it as a securing asset and separately relates Exposure to Collateral through secured_by. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / entities.Collateral Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `Collateral.definition` states collateral is an asset securing an exposure, while `Collateral.is_a: Exposure` declares Collateral as a subtype of Exposure. Source finding context: `Collateral` is simultaneously defined as a securing asset and declared to be an `Exposure`, creating an explicit class-membership conflict. Source finding context: The contradiction is internal to the present claim set: `Exposure` is defined as credit exposure to a borrower, while `Collateral` is defined as an asset that secures that exposure. The relation `Exposure -> Collateral` (`secured_by`) also treats them as separate roles. For a shared concept authority between a risk engine and reporting system, this weakens classification because collateral records can be logically admitted wherever exposure records are expected. Source finding context: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure` and model collateral only as a separate entity related to `Exposure` through `secured_by`, or introduce a separate parent such as `RiskMitigant` if inheritance is needed. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When systems interpret `is_a` as subtype inheritance or type membership, collateral instances become valid exposure instances despite being separately defined as securing assets. Source finding context: Risk engines or reports can include collateral objects in exposure-oriented logic, aggregations, or validations, undermining the shared classification contract. Source finding context: The ontology uses subtype inheritance (`is_a`) to connect a role/entity that is explicitly defined as a separate secured asset rather than a credit exposure. Source finding context: `Collateral` is declared as a subtype of `Exposure` through `is_a: Exposure`. Source finding context: `Collateral` is also defined as an asset that secures an exposure, not as the exposure itself. Source finding context: The relation set separately links `Exposure` to `Collateral` with `secured_by`, reinforcing that the claims treat them as distinct roles while inheritance collapses them into one type hierarchy.
  - affected purpose: The shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: The ontology's purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems.
  - failure condition: When systems interpret `is_a` as subtype inheritance or type membership, collateral instances become valid exposure instances despite being separately defined as securing assets.
  - impact: Collateral objects can be admitted into exposure logic, aggregation, or reporting, undermining the shared classification contract. Source finding context: Risk engines or reports can include collateral objects in exposure-oriented logic, aggregations, or validations, undermining the shared classification contract.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology uses subtype inheritance (`is_a`) to connect a role/entity that is explicitly defined as a separate secured asset rather than a credit exposure.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Exposure.definition`: 차주에 대한 신용 익스포저(대출, 한도성 여신 포함).`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Collateral.definition`: 익스포저를 담보하는 자산. 담보 평가의 기본 단위.`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Collateral.is_a: Exposure``, `Embedded Materialized Input: relation `{ from: Exposure, to: Collateral, kind: secured_by }``, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Collateral.definition` states collateral is an asset securing an exposure, while `Collateral.is_a: Exposure` declares Collateral as a subtype of Exposure.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-012`, `finding-012.cause-001`, `finding-012.cause-002`, `finding-012.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-010`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.logic`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-010`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-001`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: logic
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: The ontology is meant to serve as the shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. If is_a is interpreted as subtype inheritance or class membership, collateral instances become valid exposure instances, so downstream exposure logic, aggregation, validation, or reporting can admit collateral records where only credit exposures should appear.
  - root cause: The causal chain starts with the inheritance assertion Collateral.is_a Exposure. That assertion gives Collateral exposure membership even though the same model defines Collateral as an asset securing an exposure, not as the exposure itself.
  - causal path: The model first declares Collateral as a subtype of Exposure, then defines Collateral as a securing asset, and then separately links Exposure to Collateral with secured_by. Those claims treat Collateral and Exposure as distinct roles while inheritance collapses them into one type hierarchy, producing the observed class-membership conflict.
  - action: Remove Collateral.is_a Exposure and keep Collateral as a separate securing-asset entity related to Exposure through secured_by. If inheritance is needed, introduce or use a more appropriate parent such as RiskMitigant so collateral can share risk-mitigation behavior without becoming an exposure.

- issue-011 (medium): RiskAppetite breach logic is materially defective because it defines breach as ExposureAggregate exceeding RiskAppetite at the entity level, while the ontology only declares ExposureAggregate.total_amount and RiskAppetite.threshold as comparable numeric values.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, logic, semantics, coverage, evolution, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 6/6 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - remaining disagreement: 0/6 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: logic
  - issue statement: RiskAppetite breach logic compares ExposureAggregate to RiskAppetite as entities rather than comparing total_amount to threshold. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / RiskAppetite.definition Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `RiskAppetite.definition`: `ExposureAggregate가 RiskAppetite를 초과하면 RiskAppetite 위반이며, 위반 여부는 RiskAppetite 준수 상태로 정의된다`; attributes include `threshold` and `compliance_status`, but no amount-valued RiskAppetite field. Source finding context: The breach rule compares `ExposureAggregate` to `RiskAppetite` as an entity, while the comparable numeric attribute is `RiskAppetite.threshold`. Source finding context: The present claims declare `ExposureAggregate.total_amount` and `RiskAppetite.threshold` as numbers, but not `ExposureAggregate` or `RiskAppetite` themselves as numeric values. A greater-than comparison between an aggregate entity and an appetite entity is not simultaneously satisfiable with the declared attribute typing unless an unstated projection is assumed. In a shared engine/reporting contract, this makes the compliance rule ambiguous at the formal level where the engine needs an executable predicate and reports need the same predicate. Source finding context: Rewrite the rule as an attribute-level predicate, e.g. `RiskAppetite.compliance_status = breached iff ExposureAggregate.total_amount > RiskAppetite.threshold` for the applicable borrower/scope/as_of, and define the required relation/scope between the aggregate and appetite. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-002 Source finding context: Shared definition of risk appetite breach/compliance between risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When consumers implement the breach predicate from the ontology text, the rule compares entity objects rather than declared numeric attributes. Source finding context: Different consumers may choose different implicit projections or reject the rule as invalid, so breach status can diverge between engine and reports. Source finding context: The rule is stated at entity level even though the ontology declares the comparable quantities only as attributes. Source finding context: `RiskAppetite.definition` states the violation condition as `ExposureAggregate` exceeding `RiskAppetite`. Source finding context: The ontology declares the numeric measure for the aggregate as `ExposureAggregate.total_amount`, not as the `ExposureAggregate` entity itself. Source finding context: The ontology declares the numeric appetite value as `RiskAppetite.threshold`, not as the `RiskAppetite` entity itself.
  - affected purpose: Shared definition of risk appetite breach and compliance between risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: Shared definition of risk appetite breach/compliance between risk engine and reporting systems.
  - failure condition: When consumers implement the breach predicate from the ontology text, they must infer an unstated projection from entity objects to numeric attributes. Source finding context: When consumers implement the breach predicate from the ontology text, the rule compares entity objects rather than declared numeric attributes.
  - impact: Consumers may choose different projections or reject the rule, causing breach status to diverge between engine and reports. Source finding context: Different consumers may choose different implicit projections or reject the rule as invalid, so breach status can diverge between engine and reports.
  - root hypothesis: The risk-appetite breach rule is stated at entity level even though the ontology declares the comparable quantities only as attributes.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `ExposureAggregate.total_amount: type: number``, `Embedded Materialized Input: `RiskAppetite.threshold: type: number``, `Embedded Materialized Input: `RiskAppetite.compliance_status: enum [compliant, breached]``, `Embedded Materialized Input: `RiskAppetite.definition` comparison text`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `RiskAppetite.definition`: `ExposureAggregate가 RiskAppetite를 초과하면 RiskAppetite 위반이며, 위반 여부는 RiskAppetite 준수 상태로 정의된다`; attributes include `threshold` and `compliance_status`, but no amount-valued RiskAppetite field.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-013`, `finding-013.cause-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `RiskAppetite.definition``, `finding-013.cause-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `ExposureAggregate.attributes.total_amount.type: number``, `finding-013.cause-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `RiskAppetite.attributes.threshold.type: number``, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-011`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.logic`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-011`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-011`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-004`, `rel-011`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: logic
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens the declared purpose of sharing one risk appetite breach/compliance definition between the risk engine and reporting systems. Consumers implementing the ontology text must infer how to project entity objects into numbers, so the engine and reports may choose different projections or reject the predicate, producing divergent breach status under the same compliance term.
  - root cause: The starting point is the entity-level breach rule: RiskAppetite.definition states the violation condition as ExposureAggregate exceeding RiskAppetite, but the declared numeric quantities live on attributes. Because neither entity is declared as a numeric value, the rule cannot serve as an executable shared predicate without an unstated coercion or projection.
  - causal path: The causal path is direct: the definition compares ExposureAggregate to RiskAppetite; the aggregate’s numeric measure is declared as ExposureAggregate.total_amount; the appetite’s numeric limit is declared as RiskAppetite.threshold; and no formal comparison semantics or implicit entity-to-value coercion is provided within the boundary. That leaves the compliance_status rule formally ambiguous for consumers that need the same predicate.
  - action: Rewrite the breach rule as a scoped attribute-level predicate, for example RiskAppetite.compliance_status is breached iff the applicable ExposureAggregate.total_amount is greater than RiskAppetite.threshold. The fix must also define the relation and scope linking the aggregate to the appetite, including borrower segment, portfolio, product, currency, and as_of basis where needed, so all consumers evaluate the same rule.

- issue-014 (medium): LTV is a material ontology issue because it is exposed as a shared ratio-shaped concept without an explicit authority contract. The ontology must decide whether Exposure.ltv is a recomputable derived ratio or a manual review metric, then encode the corresponding formula/provenance, source, currency, and as-of semantics.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: coverage, semantics, axiology, evolution, logic, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 6/6 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - remaining disagreement: 0/6 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: semantics
  - issue statement: LTV is exposed as a derived ratio-shaped concept but lacks authoritative formula, source-value, currency, and valuation-time semantics. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml Exposure.ltv Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Exposure.ltv note = "담보인정비율. 심사 시점에 심사역이 입력한다."; Collateral has appraised_value and classification rule covers stale appraisals. Source finding context: LTV is named as a derived ratio but modeled as an independent manual input without source-value semantics. Source finding context: The semantics lens treats names containing ratios as derived-value risk areas when source attributes coexist. Here Collateral.appraised_value and appraised_at are present, and stale valuation affects reevaluation, but LTV is independently entered. This creates semantic drift: consumers cannot know whether LTV means current exposure-to-current-collateral value, origination-time ratio, approved collateral recognition ratio, or a manually overridden review metric. Source finding context: Either define LTV as a derived attribute with an authoritative formula, source fields, valuation timestamp, and currency basis, or rename/split it into an explicitly manual review metric with override provenance and as-of semantics. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003 Source finding context: Shared risk-engine and reporting concept basis for exposure and collateral metrics. Source finding context: When systems compare or recompute LTV using Exposure.amount and Collateral.appraised_value/appraised_at, the stored manual LTV may not mean the same ratio or time basis. Source finding context: Risk engines and reports can disagree on collateral adequacy or policy thresholds because the ontology exposes a ratio-shaped concept without authoritative derivation or temporal semantics. Source finding context: A ratio-named field is modeled as an independent manual input while likely source values and valuation timing are also modeled. Source finding context: `ltv` is defined as a number and described as a collateral recognition ratio manually entered at review time. Source finding context: The ontology also contains Exposure.amount and Collateral.appraised_value/appraised_at, which are plausible source and timing inputs for an LTV-like ratio. Source finding context: Collateral valuation age is semantically material because stale appraised_value triggers reevaluation after one year.
  - affected purpose: Shared risk-engine and reporting concept basis for exposure and collateral metrics.
  - failure condition: When systems compare or recompute LTV from Exposure.amount and Collateral.appraised_value/appraised_at, the stored manual LTV may not mean the same ratio or time basis. Source finding context: When systems compare or recompute LTV using Exposure.amount and Collateral.appraised_value/appraised_at, the stored manual LTV may not mean the same ratio or time basis.
  - impact: Risk engines and reports can disagree on collateral adequacy or thresholds because a ratio-shaped concept lacks derivation and temporal authority. Source finding context: Risk engines and reports can disagree on collateral adequacy or policy thresholds because the ontology exposes a ratio-shaped concept without authoritative derivation or temporal semantics.
  - root hypothesis: A ratio-named field is modeled as an independent manual input while likely source values and valuation timing are also modeled.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Exposure.ltv note`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Exposure.amount`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Collateral.appraised_value and appraised_at`, `Embedded Materialized Input: classification_rules stale appraisal rule`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Exposure.ltv note = "담보인정비율. 심사 시점에 심사역이 입력한다."; Collateral has appraised_value and classification rule covers stale appraisals.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-016`, `finding-016.cause-001`, `finding-016.cause-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Collateral.appraised_value`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Collateral.appraised_at`, `finding-016.cause-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: classification_rules third rule`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-014`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.logic`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-014`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.structure`, `Ontology owner decision on whether Exposure.ltv is intended as a recomputable derived ratio or a manual judgment metric.`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: follow_up, accept_risk, fix_before_release
  - materiality: This weakens the purpose of providing a shared risk-engine and reporting concept basis for exposure and collateral metrics. If different systems compare or recompute LTV from Exposure.amount and Collateral.appraised_value/appraised_at while the stored value was manually entered under a different basis, risk engines and reports can disagree on collateral adequacy or policy thresholds.
  - root cause: The starting point is that LTV is a ratio-shaped shared concept, but the ontology does not specify whether it is authoritative derivation or manual judgment. That unresolved authority choice comes before formula details: until the intended nature of the value is decided, consumers cannot know whether to derive it, trust an entered value, preserve override provenance, or compare it to collateral valuation timing.
  - causal path: The causal path begins with Exposure.ltv being defined as a number and described as a collateral recognition ratio entered by a reviewer at review time. The ontology also models plausible source and timing inputs, including Exposure.amount and Collateral.appraised_value/appraised_at, and treats stale collateral appraisals as semantically relevant through a reevaluation rule. Because these adjacent source values and timing rules exist but are not tied to LTV by formula, currency basis, valuation timestamp, or manual provenance, the same field can be interpreted as current exposure-to-current-value LTV, origination-time LTV, approved collateral recognition ratio, or reviewer override metric.
  - action: Close the issue before the next stage by making an ontology-owner decision on the intended meaning of Exposure.ltv. If it is derived, define the authoritative formula, source fields, currency basis, and valuation/as-of timestamp. If it is manual, rename or split it from recomputable LTV and add provenance, review/as-of semantics, and boundary clarity so downstream systems know not to treat it as the same derived ratio.

- issue-015 (medium): Limit utilization is not a stable shared concept because the ontology lets approved limit, available or remaining limit, utilized exposure, and FX conversion basis collapse into the same calculation surface.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: semantics
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: semantics
  - issue statement: Limit amount semantics mix approved limit, available limit, utilization denominator, and per-system currency conversion without a stable authoritative meaning. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml Limit.approved_amount and classification_rules Limit 소진율 Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Limit.approved_amount note says CRM available_limit is managed by summing; classification rule defines utilization as ExposureAggregate.total_amount / Limit.approved_amount with each system's same-day FX table. Source finding context: Limit amount semantics mix approved limit, available limit, utilization denominator, and cross-system currency conversion without a single authoritative meaning. Source finding context: Approved amount, available limit, and utilized/exposure amount are distinct credit limit concepts. Treating CRM available_limit as summable with approved_amount blurs remaining-capacity semantics with approved-capacity semantics. Because utilization depends on both denominator meaning and FX basis, allowing each system to use its own FX table further changes the semantic value of the same named utilization ratio. Source finding context: Separate approved limit, available/remaining limit, and utilized exposure as distinct concepts; define whether they may be aggregated and under what currency/as-of rules. Make the FX source and valuation date an ontology-governed authority or explicit input, not a per-system semantic choice. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004 Source finding context: Shared concept authority for limits and utilization reporting between risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When systems calculate or report limit utilization for multi-currency or CRM-integrated borrowers, they may use different denominator semantics or FX sources under the same rule name. Source finding context: Limit breach, utilization, and risk appetite reporting can diverge while appearing to follow the ontology because the key terms do not have stable meanings. Source finding context: The ontology lacks semantic separation and authority rules for approved limit, available limit, utilized exposure, and FX conversion basis. Source finding context: Limit is defined as borrower/product credit limit, and approved_amount is the approved limit recorded by the approval system. Source finding context: The same approved_amount note introduces CRM available_limit and says it is managed by summing, mixing approved capacity with available remaining capacity semantics. Source finding context: Limit utilization is defined as ExposureAggregate.total_amount divided by Limit.approved_amount, with currency conversion performed using each system's same-day FX table.
  - affected purpose: Shared concept authority for limits and utilization reporting between risk engine and reporting systems.
  - failure condition: When systems calculate utilization for multi-currency or CRM-integrated borrowers, they may use different denominator semantics or FX sources under the same rule name. Source finding context: When systems calculate or report limit utilization for multi-currency or CRM-integrated borrowers, they may use different denominator semantics or FX sources under the same rule name.
  - impact: Limit breach, utilization, and risk-appetite reporting can diverge while appearing to follow the ontology because key terms lack stable meanings. Source finding context: Limit breach, utilization, and risk appetite reporting can diverge while appearing to follow the ontology because the key terms do not have stable meanings.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology lacks semantic separation and authority rules for approved limit, available limit, utilized exposure, and FX conversion basis.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Limit.definition`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Limit.approved_amount note`, `Embedded Materialized Input: classification_rules second rule`, `Embedded Materialized Input: Limit.approved_amount note says CRM available_limit is managed by summing; classification rule defines utilization as ExposureAggregate.total_amount / Limit.approved_amount with each system's same-day FX table.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-017`, `finding-017.cause-001`, `finding-017.cause-002`, `finding-017.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-015`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.logic`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-015`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-007`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-015`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-002`, `rel-007`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-007`, `rel-015`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens shared concept authority between the risk engine and reporting systems. For multi-currency or CRM-integrated borrowers, two systems can both claim to follow the ontology while using different denominator meanings or FX tables, causing limit breach, utilization, and risk-appetite reporting to diverge.
  - root cause: The starting point is the ontology's lack of separated concepts and authority rules for approved limit, available limit, utilized exposure, and FX conversion basis. Without those distinctions, later utilization rules cannot determine which amount is authoritative, whether remaining capacity can be aggregated, or which valuation date and FX source controls conversion.
  - causal path: The causal path begins with Limit being defined as a borrower/product credit limit and approved_amount as the approved limit from the approval system. The approved_amount note then introduces CRM available_limit as managed by summing, which mixes approved capacity with remaining-capacity semantics. Finally, the utilization rule divides ExposureAggregate.total_amount by Limit.approved_amount while allowing each system's same-day FX table, so the same named ratio can vary by denominator interpretation and currency authority.
  - action: Separate approved limit, available or remaining limit, and utilized exposure as distinct ontology concepts, then explicitly govern aggregation, currency, as-of date, and FX-source rules. This must be fixed before the next stage because utilization and reporting rules depend on these meanings being stable before risk engine and reporting implementations can rely on them.

Include required scoping keys, such as borrower segment, portfolio, product, or currency, so risk-engine calculation and reporting interpretation share the same structural contract before the next stage.

  - source lenses: structure
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - materiality: This weakens the ontology's shared concept basis between risk-engine output and reporting-system classification. Consumers that need the authoritative source path for Exposure.risk_grade can only find the dependency in prose, so different systems may choose different borrower joins, precedence rules, or override behavior while still claiming to follow the ontology.
  - root cause: The causal chain starts where a cross-entity derivation was placed in classification_rules without adding the corresponding graph-level dependency. That leaves the structural model unable to express that RiskRating.grade is the source for Exposure.risk_grade, rather than merely another concept associated through the borrower context.
  - causal path: The evidence path is: Exposure.risk_grade is declared as the exposure-level report grade; RiskRating.grade is declared as the risk-engine grade; the classification rule says Exposure.risk_grade is derived from RiskRating.grade; but the relations graph has no direct Exposure-to-RiskRating derivation relation. That combination makes the required lineage observable only in unstructured rule text and underdetermines implementation behavior for graph-traversing consumers.
  - action: Add an explicit relation or typed dependency for the RiskRating.grade to Exposure.risk_grade derivation. The fix should not be a bare edge only: it must also encode or align the authoritative source path, borrower join/scoping rule, and precedence or override behavior with the broader grade-authority semantics before the next stage.

### Conditional Consensus
- issue-001 (no-deliberation-needed): Multiple rating scales coexist without an ontology-owned conversion authority, so shared grade meaning can diverge between risk-engine and reporting consumers while still appearing compliant with the ontology.
- issue-004 (narrowed): Manual Exposure.risk_grade overrides are a material issue because the ontology permits reporting-side grade changes but does not model how a reported grade resolves from an engine-derived value versus an approved override, nor does it preserve the evidence needed to audit that override.
- issue-005 (no-deliberation-needed): Time-dependent ratings, limits, mappings, and exchange-rate inputs are modeled as current or undated values, so the ontology cannot reliably reconstruct the values that were valid for a historical report or risk-engine decision.
- issue-006 (no-deliberation-needed): Values shared across the risk engine and reporting systems are not governed by a single ontology authority. Grade mappings, limit values, and exchange-rate sources are delegated to external or per-system sources, so consumers must reconcile them independently.
- issue-013 (no-deliberation-needed): Three credit-grade concepts overlap semantically but the ontology does not define an authoritative internal mapping among them. As a result, Borrower.internal_rating, RiskRating.grade, and Exposure.risk_grade can appear to participate in one shared credit-risk taxonomy while carrying different meanings across engine and reporting consumers.
- issue-008 (no-deliberation-needed): Stateful credit-risk objects are modeled without lifecycle, terminal, remediation, archival, or post-close correction concepts, so the ontology cannot consistently represent whether exposures, limits, collateral, or risk-appetite compliance records are active, closed, expired, released, remediated, or corrected.
- issue-009 (no-deliberation-needed): Core product, collateral, borrower segment, and grade classifications are modeled as closed enum literals, so ordinary business classification changes require ontology schema changes instead of governed code-set updates.
- issue-011 (narrowed): RiskAppetite breach logic is materially defective because it defines breach as ExposureAggregate exceeding RiskAppetite at the entity level, while the ontology only declares ExposureAggregate.total_amount and RiskAppetite.threshold as comparable numeric values.
- issue-014 (narrowed): LTV is a material ontology issue because it is exposed as a shared ratio-shaped concept without an explicit authority contract. The ontology must decide whether Exposure.ltv is a recomputable derived ratio or a manual review metric, then encode the corresponding formula/provenance, source, currency, and as-of semantics.
- issue-015 (no-deliberation-needed): Limit utilization is not a stable shared concept because the ontology lets approved limit, available or remaining limit, utilized exposure, and FX conversion basis collapse into the same calculation surface.
- issue-016 (no-deliberation-needed): RiskAppetite is a material structural gap: the ontology defines it as the compliance threshold for ExposureAggregate, but the relation graph never connects RiskAppetite to the aggregate or scope it governs.
- issue-017 (narrowed): Issue-017 is a material structural lineage gap: Exposure.risk_grade is described as derived from RiskRating.grade, but the ontology graph does not encode that derivation as an explicit relation or typed dependency.

### Disagreement
- none

### Axiology-Proposed Additional Perspectives
- issue-001 (high): Multiple rating scales coexist without an ontology-owned conversion authority, so shared grade meaning can diverge between risk-engine and reporting consumers while still appearing compliant with the ontology.
- issue-002 (high): Limit utilization is materially under-specified because the ontology defines a shared risk/reporting formula without making its calculation time, data freshness, FX basis, numerator, denominator, and CRM or external limit-source inputs authoritative parts of the contract.
- issue-003 (medium): Default and delinquency status are excluded from the shared credit-risk ontology without a compensating boundary concept or relation. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / scope notes excluding default status Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: notes[1] says default/delinquency status is outside this ontology because a separate delinquency-management system handles it; the header says the ontology is for credit-risk management and shared risk/reporting concepts. Source finding context: Excluding default status from a credit-risk shared ontology leaves a purpose-critical stakeholder boundary undefined. Source finding context: Boundary exclusions can be valid, but for credit risk management, default/delinquency status is central to the meaning and operational use of risk grades, exposure control, and reports. A shared ontology can delegate ownership to another system while still defining the boundary concept and relation. Here the exclusion is accepted without a compensating interface, which risks purpose drift: the ontology optimizes scope neatness over the completeness needed for shared risk interpretation. Source finding context: Keep delinquency/default ownership outside if needed, but add a boundary concept or relation such as `DefaultStatus`/`DelinquencyStatus` with source authority, join key, as-of time, and effects on ratings, exposure classification, limit compliance, and reporting eligibility. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-003 Source finding context: The ontology's declared credit-risk management purpose and its role as a shared basis for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When a borrower or exposure has delinquency/default state in the external system, but risk engine or reporting consumers rely on this ontology without a defined boundary relation to that state. Source finding context: Core credit-risk outputs can omit or inconsistently join a purpose-critical status, weakening shared interpretation across stakeholders even if another system owns the source data. Source finding context: The ontology treats an ownership boundary as a scope exclusion instead of modeling the boundary relation for a purpose-critical risk concept. Source finding context: The ontology is scoped as a credit-risk management concept model for shared engine/reporting use. Source finding context: The ontology excludes default/delinquency status entirely because a separate system handles it. Source finding context: No visible entity or relation in the bounded target links borrower, exposure, rating, limit, or report concepts to the external default/delinquency status. Source finding context: This leaves a core risk-status boundary undefined for systems expected to share concepts through the ontology.

### Immediate Actions Required
- issue-001 (high)
  - issue: Multiple rating scales coexist without an ontology-owned conversion authority, so shared grade meaning can diverge between risk-engine and reporting consumers while still appearing compliant with the ontology.
  - target: The ontology lets multiple rating scales coexist without an ontology-owned conversion authority, weakening shared grade meaning and change continuity across risk engine and reporting consumers. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / classification_rules[0] and notes[0] Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: target header states the ontology is a shared authority document for risk engine and reports; classification_rules[0] says `Exposure.risk_grade` is derived from `RiskRating.grade` but can be manually adjusted by the reporting team; notes[0] says the three rating scales coexist and conversion is performed by each consuming system. Source finding context: Shared concept authority is weakened by delegating rating-scale conversion and report-grade override authority to consumers. Source finding context: For an ontology whose stated purpose is to provide shared conceptual authority, the rating concepts are purpose-critical because risk engines and reports both depend on them. Letting each consumer perform its own conversion, while also allowing one consumer group to override a derived grade, creates ungoverned local authority over a core risk classification. This is a value/purpose drift: local reporting flexibility is accepted without a declared governance seat, precedence rule, audit trail, or canonical mapping, so the shared basis can diverge exactly where shared meaning is most needed. Source finding context: Make one canonical authority path for rating conversion and overrides: define the authoritative mapping among the three grade scales in the ontology or a governed referenced artifact, specify precedence between engine-derived and manually adjusted grades, and require override reason, actor, timestamp/as_of, and audit status if report-grade adjustments remain allowed. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's declared purpose as the shared concept authority for risk engines and reporting systems. Source finding context: When the engine, reporting team, or another consumer maps rating scales differently, or when reporting manually adjusts `Exposure.risk_grade` without canonical precedence and audit semantics. Source finding context: The same borrower or exposure can carry different risk classifications across engine and reporting outputs, weakening trust in risk decisions and reports produced from the supposedly shared ontology. Source finding context: The ontology does not assign canonical authority, precedence, or governance for translating and overriding its core rating concepts. Source finding context: The target states that the ontology is intended to be the shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: The target contains three coexisting grade scales and says conversion is performed by each consuming system. Source finding context: The reporting team may manually adjust a grade that is otherwise said to be derived from engine output. Source finding context: These local conversions and adjustments can produce divergent risk classifications under a document whose purpose is shared authority. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / rating scale model Source finding context: materialized-input.md: Borrower.internal_rating note says "1~10 숫자 등급 체계"; RiskRating.grade enum values [R1, R2, R3, R4, R5] says mapping table is in the risk-team wiki; Exposure.risk_grade enum values [AAA, AA, A, BBB, BB, B, CCC]; notes say the three borrower grade scales coexist and each consumer system performs conversion. Source finding context: 등급 체계가 온톨로지 내부에서 버전 있는 변환 개념으로 모델링되지 않아 등급 체계 변경 시 소비 시스템별 재해석이 필요하다. Source finding context: evolution 관점에서 변경 내성의 핵심은 외부 표준이나 내부 등급 정책이 바뀌어도 기존 데이터와 규칙의 연속성이 유지되는지다. 현재 증거는 세 스케일의 병존과 소비자별 변환을 명시하므로, 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 같은 차주/익스포저를 다르게 매핑할 수 있고 새 스케일 도입 시 모든 소비 시스템의 변환 로직을 동시에 수정해야 한다. 이는 공유 개념 기준이라는 목적을 직접 약화한다. Source finding context: 등급 스케일을 독립 개념으로 승격하고 scale_id, grade_code, effective_from/effective_to, version, source_authority를 둔다. RiskRating.grade와 Exposure.risk_grade는 원천 등급과 보고 등급을 구분해 참조하게 하고, 변환은 온톨로지 안의 RatingMapping 엔티티 또는 규칙 세트로 버전 관리한다. Source finding context: 등급 단계가 추가되거나 기존 R1~R5, 1~10, AAA~CCC 중 하나가 개편될 때 변환 권위가 온톨로지 밖의 위키와 소비 시스템별 구현으로 분산된다. Source finding context: 대상은 세 개의 등급 표현을 별도 속성으로 선언한다. Source finding context: RiskRating.grade의 Borrower.internal_rating 매핑은 온톨로지 내부가 아니라 리스크팀 위키를 참조한다. The fix should define authoritative mappings among the three scales, source authority, effective_from/effective_to or version fields, precedence between engine-derived and reporting-adjusted grades, and audit semantics for any allowed override, including reason, actor, timestamp/as_of, and audit status. - issue-002 (high)
  - issue: Limit utilization is materially under-specified because the ontology defines a shared risk/reporting formula without making its calculation time, data freshness, FX basis, numerator, denominator, and CRM or external limit-source inputs authoritative parts of the contract.
  - target: The utilization formula relies on stale or system-specific inputs without making calculation time, freshness, FX authority, and CRM limit contribution part of the ontology contract. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / ExposureAggregate and Limit utilization rule Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `ExposureAggregate.total_amount` is stored from a month-end batch and intraday new loans are excluded until the next batch; `classification_rules[1]` defines limit utilization as `ExposureAggregate.total_amount / Limit.approved_amount`; `Limit.approved_amount` notes CRM available_limit is jointly managed. Source finding context: A stale batch aggregate is used as the authoritative numerator for limit utilization without temporal freshness boundaries. Source finding context: The declared purpose is a shared concept basis for risk engine and reporting. A limit utilization concept used for credit risk control is purpose-critical, but the model bakes in a known temporal lag without naming it as a stale snapshot, without defining when it is valid for decisions, and without separating reporting snapshot semantics from real-time risk-control semantics. This accepts a hidden tradeoff: operational convenience of batch storage over timely risk appetite and limit visibility. Source finding context: Define separate concepts for batch reporting exposure and decision-current exposure, require `as_of`/calculation timestamp and source freshness on both numerator and denominator, and constrain limit-utilization use by context, for example `reporting_utilization` versus `decision_utilization`. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-002 Source finding context: The ontology's purpose as a shared concept basis for risk engine and reporting, especially for limit utilization and risk exposure controls. Source finding context: When intraday lending or CRM limit changes occur after the month-end aggregate but before the next batch, and systems use the ontology's limit-utilization formula as if it represented current exposure. Source finding context: Risk engine or reports can understate utilization and risk appetite pressure, making the shared ontology operationally misleading for control decisions or cross-system reconciliation. Source finding context: The ontology does not separate reporting snapshots from decision-current exposure/limit semantics despite using the stale aggregate in a control formula. Source finding context: The target defines borrower total exposure as an aggregate of all exposure amounts. Source finding context: The stored aggregate is only calculated by month-end batch and omits intraday new lending until the next batch. Source finding context: The ontology uses that stored aggregate directly as the numerator for limit utilization. Source finding context: The denominator is also cross-system because `Limit.approved_amount` is jointly managed with CRM `available_limit`, but no common freshness or as-of rule is defined for the utilization calculation. Source finding context: A known-lag aggregate can therefore be interpreted as current utilization by the shared risk/reporting ontology. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / exposure aggregation and limit-utilization rule Source finding context: materialized-input.md: ExposureAggregate.total_amount note says monthly batch storage excludes intraday new loans until next batch; classification rule says currency conversion uses each system's same-day FX table; Limit.approved_amount note says CRM available_limit is jointly managed. Source finding context: 한도 소진율과 총익스포저 계산이 계산시점·환율권위·외부 한도 권위를 버전 있는 입력으로 고정하지 않아 신규 데이터 주기나 환율 정책 변경에 취약하다. - issue-004 (high)
  - issue: Manual Exposure.risk_grade overrides are a material issue because the ontology permits reporting-side grade changes but does not model how a reported grade resolves from an engine-derived value versus an approved override, nor does it preserve the evidence needed to audit that override.
  - target: Manual risk-grade overrides are authorized but lack audit evidence concepts for actor, time, reason, approval, and source-versus-adjusted values. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / classification_rules: Exposure.risk_grade manual adjustment Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: classification_rules[0] says Exposure.risk_grade is derived from RiskRating.grade but the reporting team may manually adjust it based on market conditions; Exposure.risk_grade has only the adjusted enum value and no adjustment evidence attributes or entity. Source finding context: Manual risk-grade overrides are in scope but the ontology lacks audit evidence concepts for the override action. Source finding context: A shared concept authority for a risk engine and reporting system must let both systems distinguish engine-derived grades from manually adjusted reported grades. The target explicitly allows manual adjustment, while the only modeled grade-bearing attributes are Borrower.internal_rating, RiskRating.grade, and Exposure.risk_grade. None of the entities or relations capture an override action, actor, timestamp, rationale, approval, or prior/current grade pair, so the reporting value cannot be reconstructed or audited from the ontology itself. Source finding context: Add a bounded override/audit concept such as RiskGradeAdjustment with exposure_ref, source_rating_ref or source_grade, adjusted_grade, actor, adjusted_at, reason/market_basis, approval_ref/status, and effective period; relate Exposure.risk_grade to the latest effective approved adjustment or the derived RiskRating mapping when no adjustment exists. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology is intended as a shared concept authority for the risk engine and reporting system. Source finding context: When Exposure.risk_grade is manually changed by the reporting team, downstream users cannot tell whether a reported grade is engine-derived or adjusted, nor reconstruct the actor, time, and basis for the reported value. Source finding context: This weakens auditability and cross-system trust for a core risk classification value, especially because the target itself authorizes manual changes to that value. Source finding context: The ontology models adjusted risk grade as a bare Exposure attribute while placing the manual adjustment rule only in free-text classification_rules. Source finding context: Exposure.risk_grade is represented as a single enum attribute on Exposure. Source finding context: The classification rule permits the reporting team to manually adjust that value based on market conditions. Source finding context: No entity or attribute captures the manual adjustment action, actor, timestamp, basis, approval, or source-versus-adjusted value.
  - failure condition: When reporting manually changes Exposure.risk_grade, downstream users cannot reconstruct whether, when, why, or by whom the value was adjusted. Source finding context: When Exposure.risk_grade is manually changed by the reporting team, downstream users cannot tell whether a reported grade is engine-derived or adjusted, nor reconstruct the actor, time, and basis for the reported value.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Add a bounded RiskGradeAdjustment or equivalent value-resolution concept before the next stage. It should connect the exposure to the source RiskRating or source grade, adjusted grade, actor, adjusted_at time, reason or market basis, approval reference/status, and effective period, and define that Exposure.risk_grade resolves to the latest effective approved adjustment when present or to the derived RiskRating mapping otherwise.
  - remediation: Add a bounded RiskGradeAdjustment or equivalent value-resolution concept before the next stage. It should connect the exposure to the source RiskRating or source grade, adjusted grade, actor, adjusted_at time, reason or market basis, approval reference/status, and effective period, and define that Exposure.risk_grade resolves to the latest effective approved adjustment when present or to the derived RiskRating mapping otherwise.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-005 (high)
  - issue: Time-dependent ratings, limits, mappings, and exchange-rate inputs are modeled as current or undated values, so the ontology cannot reliably reconstruct the values that were valid for a historical report or risk-engine decision.
  - target: Time-dependent ratings, limits, mappings, and exchange rates lack validity periods or history, preventing point-in-time reconstruction. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / rating and exchange-rate dependent values Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: RiskRating.grade has no effective date; Borrower.internal_rating has no time fields; Exposure.risk_grade has no effective date; classification_rules[1] uses each system's same-day exchange-rate table, but no exchange-rate concept exists. Source finding context: Time-dependent classification inputs are modeled without validity periods or history, preventing point-in-time reconstruction. Source finding context: The review target is a shared concept basis for risk calculation and reporting. For historical reports or replayed risk-engine decisions, both systems need the value that was valid at the relevant point in time. The target explicitly mentions monthly batch aggregate timing and same-day exchange-rate tables, but the ontology lacks effective_from/effective_to, as_of, version, or source-table references for several time-dependent inputs, so historical utilization and grade outputs cannot be reconstructed from the concept model. Source finding context: Introduce temporal validity consistently for time-dependent concepts: add effective_from/effective_to or as_of/version fields to RiskRating, Borrower rating, Exposure.risk_grade, Limit.approved_amount, and any mapping table; add an ExchangeRate or CurrencyConversionRate concept with source_system/table authority, currency pair, rate_date/time, and rate value. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002 Source finding context: The ontology is meant to support shared risk-engine and reporting interpretation of credit-risk values. Source finding context: For a prior reporting date or risk decision, the systems need to reconstruct the rating, limit, mapping, and exchange-rate values valid at that time, but the ontology only stores current or undated values for several inputs. Source finding context: This can make historical reports non-reproducible and risk calculations inconsistent across systems because point-in-time authority is absent for core classification inputs. Source finding context: Temporal coverage is applied only selectively rather than as a concept dimension for all point-in-time risk inputs. Source finding context: Some concepts include point-in-time markers, such as Collateral.appraised_at and ExposureAggregate.as_of. Source finding context: Other time-dependent values, including ratings, risk grades, approved limits, and exchange rates, lack equivalent validity/history fields or entities. Source finding context: Because classification and utilization calculations depend on those values, historical outputs cannot be reconstructed from the ontology alone.
  - failure condition: For a prior reporting date or risk decision, systems need the rating, limit, mapping, or FX value valid at that time, but the ontology only provides current or undated values. Source finding context: For a prior reporting date or risk decision, the systems need to reconstruct the rating, limit, mapping, and exchange-rate values valid at that time, but the ontology only stores current or undated values for several inputs.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Add temporal validity semantics consistently to all time-dependent risk inputs, using effective_from/effective_to, as_of, version, or equivalent history fields where appropriate. Also introduce an authoritative ExchangeRate or CurrencyConversionRate concept that records source system or table authority, currency pair, rate date or time, and rate value. This should be fixed before the next stage because downstream classification, utilization, reporting, and replay logic depend on these values having point-in-time authority.
  - remediation: Add temporal validity semantics consistently to all time-dependent risk inputs, using effective_from/effective_to, as_of, version, or equivalent history fields where appropriate. Also introduce an authoritative ExchangeRate or CurrencyConversionRate concept that records source system or table authority, currency pair, rate date or time, and rate value. This should be fixed before the next stage because downstream classification, utilization, reporting, and replay logic depend on these values having point-in-time authority.

- issue-006 (high)
  - issue: Values shared across the risk engine and reporting systems are not governed by a single ontology authority. Grade mappings, limit values, and exchange-rate sources are delegated to external or per-system sources, so consumers must reconcile them independently.
  - target: Values shared across systems lack a single authority concept, leaving consumers to reconcile grade mappings, limits, and FX sources themselves. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / multi-system managed values and grade scales Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: RiskRating.grade note sends mapping to a risk-team wiki; Limit.approved_amount note says approval system records it and CRM available_limit is managed together; classification_rules[1] uses each system's exchange-rate table; notes say each consuming system performs grade-scale conversion. Source finding context: Values explicitly shared across systems lack a single authority concept, leaving consumers to reconcile competing sources themselves. Source finding context: Coverage of single authority is required when values are managed across systems. The target's declared purpose is to be a shared concept authority, but several fields are explicitly delegated to external or per-consumer sources without an authoritative entity, relation, or precedence rule. That omission means the risk engine and reporting system can legally produce different grade mappings, limit utilization, or currency-converted values while still conforming to the ontology text. Source finding context: Add authority-bearing concepts or attributes for each parallel value: a canonical RatingScaleMapping with owner/version/effective period; a LimitAuthority or LimitSnapshot separating approved_amount from CRM available_limit with source_system and precedence; and a governed ExchangeRateSource/ExchangeRate table. State which source controls each calculation and how conflicts are resolved. Source finding context: When systems disagree on grade conversion, available limit, or exchange rate, the ontology provides no authoritative source or precedence rule to resolve the discrepancy. Source finding context: The shared ontology fails its authority purpose for central risk/reporting calculations and allows inconsistent but ontology-compliant outputs across consuming systems. Source finding context: The ontology acknowledges cross-system coexistence but does not promote mapping, limit availability, or exchange-rate authority into governed ontology concepts. Source finding context: The target declares multiple system-specific sources for grade conversion, limits, and exchange rates. Source finding context: No entity or relation names a canonical source, precedence rule, owner, version, or conflict-resolution concept for those values. Source finding context: Therefore the risk engine and reporting system can each use different external mappings or rates while remaining inside the documented ontology.
  - failure condition: When systems disagree on grade conversion, available limit, or exchange rate, the ontology provides no canonical source or precedence rule to resolve the discrepancy. Source finding context: When systems disagree on grade conversion, available limit, or exchange rate, the ontology provides no authoritative source or precedence rule to resolve the discrepancy.

  - rationale: Add authority-bearing concepts for each shared value class: a canonical RatingScaleMapping with owner, version, and effective period; a LimitAuthority or LimitSnapshot that separates approved_amount from CRM available_limit and records source_system plus precedence; and a governed ExchangeRateSource or ExchangeRate table. The ontology should state which source controls each calculation and how conflicts are resolved before the next stage, because this issue is framed as a next-step blocker that must close before release-quality use.
  - remediation: Add authority-bearing concepts for each shared value class: a canonical RatingScaleMapping with owner, version, and effective period; a LimitAuthority or LimitSnapshot that separates approved_amount from CRM available_limit and records source_system plus precedence; and a governed ExchangeRateSource or ExchangeRate table. The ontology should state which source controls each calculation and how conflicts are resolved before the next stage, because this issue is framed as a next-step blocker that must close before release-quality use.

- issue-012 (high)
  - issue: Collateral is wrongly modeled as a subtype of Exposure. The ontology should treat Collateral as a separate securing-asset entity and connect it to Exposure through the existing secured_by relationship instead of inheritance.
  - target: Collateral is semantically misclassified as an Exposure subtype, creating a foundational type boundary error. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml entities.Collateral Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Collateral.definition = "익스포저를 담보하는 자산. 담보 평가의 기본 단위." and Collateral.is_a = Exposure Source finding context: Collateral is semantically misclassified as an Exposure subtype. Source finding context: The embedded target defines Exposure as credit exposure to a borrower, while Collateral is defined as an asset that secures an exposure. These are different ontological types: a credit obligation/position versus a securing asset. Because the artifact is intended as a shared concept authority for risk engines and reporting systems, this subtype assertion can cause consumers to inherit exposure semantics for collateral records and misinterpret collateral assets as exposure instances. Source finding context: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure`; keep Collateral as a separate entity and express the relationship through `Exposure secured_by Collateral` or an equivalent association with explicit cardinality/role. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's declared purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems covering exposures, grades, limits, and collateral. Source finding context: When a consuming engine or report treats subtype assertions as inheritance or classification authority, Collateral becomes an Exposure instance despite the definitions distinguishing collateral assets from credit exposures. Source finding context: This weakens trust in the shared taxonomy because a foundational entity boundary is semantically wrong, allowing collateral records to be counted, filtered, validated, or reported under exposure semantics. Source finding context: The ontology uses subtype inheritance for Collateral even though its own definitions only support an associative securing relationship to Exposure. Source finding context: Collateral is asserted as a subtype of Exposure via `is_a: Exposure`. Source finding context: The definitions distinguish Exposure as a credit exposure to a borrower and Collateral as an asset that secures an exposure. Source finding context: The same model already has an associative relation `Exposure secured_by Collateral`, so the subtype assertion is not needed to express the securing relationship.
  - failure condition: When consumers treat subtype assertions as classification authority, Collateral becomes an Exposure instance despite definitions distinguishing collateral assets from credit exposures. Source finding context: When a consuming engine or report treats subtype assertions as inheritance or classification authority, Collateral becomes an Exposure instance despite the definitions distinguishing collateral assets from credit exposures.
  - candidates: fix_now
  - rationale: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure`, keep Collateral as an independent securing-asset entity, and retain or clarify the explicit `Exposure secured_by Collateral` association with appropriate role and cardinality. This fixes the type boundary at the source while preserving the valid business relationship between exposures and collateral.
  - remediation: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure`, keep Collateral as an independent securing-asset entity, and retain or clarify the explicit `Exposure secured_by Collateral` association with appropriate role and cardinality. This fixes the type boundary at the source while preserving the valid business relationship between exposures and collateral.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing

  - issue: Three credit-grade concepts overlap semantically but the ontology does not define an authoritative internal mapping among them. As a result, Borrower.internal_rating, RiskRating.grade, and Exposure.risk_grade can appear to participate in one shared credit-risk taxonomy while carrying different meanings across engine and reporting consumers.
  - target: Three credit-grade concepts overlap semantically but lack an authoritative ontology-internal mapping. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml rating concepts Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Borrower.internal_rating values are described as 1~10; RiskRating.grade values are [R1, R2, R3, R4, R5]; Exposure.risk_grade values are [AAA, AA, A, BBB, BB, B, CCC]; notes say the three scales coexist and each consuming system performs conversion. Source finding context: Three credit-grade concepts are semantically overlapping but lack an authoritative mapping inside the ontology. Source finding context: All three fields name a borrower/exposure credit risk grade, but their scale names and authorities differ. The target itself says this artifact is meant to be the shared concept authority between a risk engine and reporting systems; leaving semantically overlapping grades to separate consumer-side mappings means the same borrower or exposure risk meaning can diverge by system. Source finding context: Define canonical grade concepts and authority boundaries in the ontology: either model one canonical rating with explicit scale mappings/versioning, or split the concepts by meaning such as borrower internal rating, engine borrower rating, and report exposure grade with authoritative mapping rules and allowed override semantics. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002 Source finding context: Shared concept authority for the risk engine and reporting system, especially for ratings used in classification and reports. Source finding context: When the engine emits RiskRating.grade and reporting consumes Exposure.risk_grade or Borrower.internal_rating, each consumer may apply a different conversion or manual override without an ontology-governed semantic mapping. Source finding context: The ontology cannot reliably establish whether two systems are referring to the same risk grade meaning, so risk classification and reporting can disagree while still appearing compliant with the shared taxonomy. Source finding context: The ontology defines multiple overlapping rating scales but delegates conversion authority to external wiki/consumer systems instead of making the semantic mapping part of the shared concept model. Source finding context: Borrower.internal_rating, RiskRating.grade, and Exposure.risk_grade all represent credit or risk grade concepts with different scales. Source finding context: The classification rule derives Exposure.risk_grade from RiskRating.grade but also allows report-team manual adjustment based on market conditions. Source finding context: The notes state that conversion among the three coexisting scales is performed by each consuming system.

Source finding context: This absence prevents consistent representation of active versus terminal, remediated, released, or corrected records.

- issue-010 (medium)
  - issue: Collateral is incorrectly modeled as an Exposure subtype even though the ontology defines it as a securing asset and already relates Exposure to Collateral through secured_by. This creates a material type-boundary contradiction that should be fixed before the ontology is used as a shared authority.
  - target: Collateral is declared as an Exposure subtype even though the ontology defines it as a securing asset and separately relates Exposure to Collateral through secured_by. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / entities.Collateral Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `Collateral.definition` states collateral is an asset securing an exposure, while `Collateral.is_a: Exposure` declares Collateral as a subtype of Exposure. Source finding context: `Collateral` is simultaneously defined as a securing asset and declared to be an `Exposure`, creating an explicit class-membership conflict. Source finding context: The contradiction is internal to the present claim set: `Exposure` is defined as credit exposure to a borrower, while `Collateral` is defined as an asset that secures that exposure. The relation `Exposure -> Collateral` (`secured_by`) also treats them as separate roles. For a shared concept authority between a risk engine and reporting system, this weakens classification because collateral records can be logically admitted wherever exposure records are expected. Source finding context: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure` and model collateral only as a separate entity related to `Exposure` through `secured_by`, or introduce a separate parent such as `RiskMitigant` if inheritance is needed. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When systems interpret `is_a` as subtype inheritance or type membership, collateral instances become valid exposure instances despite being separately defined as securing assets. Source finding context: Risk engines or reports can include collateral objects in exposure-oriented logic, aggregations, or validations, undermining the shared classification contract. Source finding context: The ontology uses subtype inheritance (`is_a`) to connect a role/entity that is explicitly defined as a separate secured asset rather than a credit exposure. Source finding context: `Collateral` is declared as a subtype of `Exposure` through `is_a: Exposure`. Source finding context: `Collateral` is also defined as an asset that secures an exposure, not as the exposure itself. Source finding context: The relation set separately links `Exposure` to `Collateral` with `secured_by`, reinforcing that the claims treat them as distinct roles while inheritance collapses them into one type hierarchy.
  - failure condition: When systems interpret `is_a` as subtype inheritance or type membership, collateral instances become valid exposure instances despite being separately defined as securing assets.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Remove Collateral.is_a Exposure and keep Collateral as a separate securing-asset entity related to Exposure through secured_by. If inheritance is needed, introduce or use a more appropriate parent such as RiskMitigant so collateral can share risk-mitigation behavior without becoming an exposure.
  - remediation: Remove Collateral.is_a Exposure and keep Collateral as a separate securing-asset entity related to Exposure through secured_by. If inheritance is needed, introduce or use a more appropriate parent such as RiskMitigant so collateral can share risk-mitigation behavior without becoming an exposure.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing

In a shared engine/reporting contract, this makes the compliance rule ambiguous at the formal level where the engine needs an executable predicate and reports need the same predicate. Source finding context: The rule is stated at entity level even though the ontology declares the comparable quantities only as attributes. Source finding context: `RiskAppetite.definition` states the violation condition as `ExposureAggregate` exceeding `RiskAppetite`. Source finding context: The ontology declares the numeric measure for the aggregate as `ExposureAggregate.total_amount`, not as the `ExposureAggregate` entity itself. Source finding context: The ontology declares the numeric appetite value as `RiskAppetite.threshold`, not as the `RiskAppetite` entity itself.
  - failure condition: When consumers implement the breach predicate from the ontology text, they must infer an unstated projection from entity objects to numeric attributes. Source finding context: When consumers implement the breach predicate from the ontology text, the rule compares entity objects rather than declared numeric attributes.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Rewrite the breach rule as a scoped attribute-level predicate, for example RiskAppetite.compliance_status is breached iff the applicable ExposureAggregate.total_amount is greater than RiskAppetite.threshold. The fix must also define the relation and scope linking the aggregate to the appetite, including borrower segment, portfolio, product, currency, and as_of basis where needed, so all consumers evaluate the same rule.
  - remediation: Rewrite the breach rule as a scoped attribute-level predicate, for example RiskAppetite.compliance_status is breached iff the applicable ExposureAggregate.total_amount is greater than RiskAppetite.threshold. The fix must also define the relation and scope linking the aggregate to the appetite, including borrower segment, portfolio, product, currency, and as_of basis where needed, so all consumers evaluate the same rule.
