Source finding context: A cross-entity derivation rule was added under classification_rules without a corresponding relation/dependency in the ontology graph. Source finding context: Exposure.risk_grade is declared as the exposure-level report grade. Source finding context: RiskRating.grade is declared as the risk-engine grade. Source finding context: The classification rule says Exposure.risk_grade is derived from RiskRating.grade, but the relations block has no direct relation between Exposure and RiskRating.
  - failure condition: When consumers need the authoritative source path for Exposure.risk_grade, the graph lacks a direct derivation relation from RiskRating.grade. Source finding context: When a consumer must determine the authoritative source path for Exposure.risk_grade, the graph lacks a direct derivation relation from RiskRating.grade.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - rationale: Add an explicit relation or typed dependency for the RiskRating.grade to Exposure.risk_grade derivation. The fix should not be a bare edge only: it must also encode or align the authoritative source path, borrower join/scoping rule, and precedence or override behavior with the broader grade-authority semantics before the next stage.
  - remediation: Add an explicit relation or typed dependency for the RiskRating.grade to Exposure.risk_grade derivation. The fix should not be a bare edge only: it must also encode or align the authoritative source path, borrower join/scoping rule, and precedence or override behavior with the broader grade-authority semantics before the next stage.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing

### Recommendations
- issue-003 (medium): Default and delinquency status are excluded from the shared credit-risk ontology without a compensating boundary concept or relation. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / scope notes excluding default status Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: notes[1] says default/delinquency status is outside this ontology because a separate delinquency-management system handles it; the header says the ontology is for credit-risk management and shared risk/reporting concepts. Source finding context: Excluding default status from a credit-risk shared ontology leaves a purpose-critical stakeholder boundary undefined. Source finding context: Boundary exclusions can be valid, but for credit risk management, default/delinquency status is central to the meaning and operational use of risk grades, exposure control, and reports. A shared ontology can delegate ownership to another system while still defining the boundary concept and relation. Here the exclusion is accepted without a compensating interface, which risks purpose drift: the ontology optimizes scope neatness over the completeness needed for shared risk interpretation. Source finding context: Keep delinquency/default ownership outside if needed, but add a boundary concept or relation such as `DefaultStatus`/`DelinquencyStatus` with source authority, join key, as-of time, and effects on ratings, exposure classification, limit compliance, and reporting eligibility. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-003 Source finding context: The ontology's declared credit-risk management purpose and its role as a shared basis for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When a borrower or exposure has delinquency/default state in the external system, but risk engine or reporting consumers rely on this ontology without a defined boundary relation to that state. Source finding context: Core credit-risk outputs can omit or inconsistently join a purpose-critical status, weakening shared interpretation across stakeholders even if another system owns the source data. Source finding context: The ontology treats an ownership boundary as a scope exclusion instead of modeling the boundary relation for a purpose-critical risk concept. Source finding context: The ontology is scoped as a credit-risk management concept model for shared engine/reporting use. Source finding context: The ontology excludes default/delinquency status entirely because a separate system handles it. Source finding context: No visible entity or relation in the bounded target links borrower, exposure, rating, limit, or report concepts to the external default/delinquency status. Source finding context: This leaves a core risk-status boundary undefined for systems expected to share concepts through the ontology.
- issue-007 (medium): Default/delinquency risk status is intentionally excluded despite being central to shared credit-risk reporting and lifecycle interpretation. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / credit risk domain scope Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: notes[1] excludes delinquency/default status as outside scope; entity list contains Borrower, Exposure, Collateral, RiskRating, Limit, ExposureAggregate, RiskAppetite only. Source finding context: Default/delinquency risk status is excluded despite being a central credit-risk lifecycle area, leaving the ontology under-covered for credit-risk reporting. Source finding context: From a coverage perspective, a credit-risk classification ontology shared by risk and reporting needs at least a conceptual place for adverse credit states, even if operational ownership belongs to another system. The target defines exposure, ratings, limits, collateral, aggregate exposure, and risk appetite, but omits the lifecycle/outcome states that determine whether credit risk has materialized. Because the note makes the omission intentional, the gap is inside the declared target boundary rather than merely unknown external detail. Source finding context: Add a minimal externally-owned CreditRiskStatus or DelinquencyDefaultStatus projection with borrower/exposure reference, status enum, source_system=delinquency management, as_of/effective period, and lifecycle states such as current, delinquent, defaulted, cured, written_off, closed, or explicitly define why reports and engines must never consume that status through this ontology. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-1bb88da9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004 Source finding context: The ontology presents itself as a credit-risk classification model for lending risk management and shared risk/reporting concepts. Source finding context: Risk reports or engine classifications that need to combine ratings/exposure with delinquency or default status cannot do so through the shared ontology. Source finding context: The ontology covers pre-default exposure and rating concepts but lacks a central outcome/lifecycle dimension, which can fragment credit-risk reporting across systems. Source finding context: The ontology treats system ownership of delinquency/default handling as a reason to omit the concept from the shared classification model. Source finding context: The target explicitly states delinquency/default status is outside the ontology scope because another system handles it. Source finding context: No replacement projection, reference, or lifecycle status concept is modeled in the entity or relation set. Source finding context: As a result, the shared credit-risk model cannot cover materialized credit-risk state across the risk/reporting boundary.
